under the action of the renormalization, i.e. to commute with the operation of taking special Birkhoff sums
(see Lemma
6.7
for a precise statement).

The strategy to construct the sequence
P^{(k)},k\in{\mathbb{N}}
of equivariant correcting operators is the following:

(1)

As first approximation of the correction operators, consider, for
k\in\mathbb{N}
, the mean value projections
\mathcal{M}^{(k)}:{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})\to\Gamma^{(k)}
, as defined in §
4.4
, and the associated correcting operators
P^{(k)}_{0}:=I-\mathcal{M}^{(k)}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
;

(2)

The correcting operators
P^{(k)}_{0}
,
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
, are not equivariant and do not take values in the quotient. Let us hence modify them by subtracting a term
\Delta^{(k)}
and composing with the projection
U^{(k)}
to the quotient space
\Gamma^{(k)}/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}
, namely consider, for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, a operator of the form
P^{(k)}:=U^{(k)}\circ P^{(k)}_{0}-\Delta^{(k)}
;

(3)

Following
[
44
]
, one can see that for
P^{(0)}
defined as in
(2)
to be equivariant, one needs to define
\Delta^{(0)}\varphi
so that the modified correction operator
U\circ\mathcal{M}^{(0)}+\Delta^{(0)}
is the limit (if it exists) of the sequence
U\circ Q(k)^{-1}\circ\mathcal{M}^{(k)}\circ S(k)(\varphi)
, which is obtained by ’
bringing back
’ the correction of
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(0)}_{\alpha})
at time
k
, namely of the function
S(k)(\varphi)
, to time
0
by applying
Q(k)^{-1}
;

(4)

Show that the sequence in
(3)
converges, so that one can define the modification operator
\Delta^{(k)}
, then the correcting operator
P^{(k)}=U^{(k)}\circ P^{(k)}_{0}-\Delta^{(k)}

has the required covariance and growths properties.

Thus, to obtain the desired correction operator one has to show that the sequence

U\circ Q(k)^{-1}\circ\mathcal{M}^{(k)}\circ S(k)(\varphi)\in H(\pi^{(0)})/\Gamma_{s}^{(0)},\qquad k\in\mathbb{N}

obtained in
(3)
converges for every
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(0)}_{\alpha})
.
Notice that when
\mathcal{M}^{(k)}
takes values in
H(\pi^{(k)})\subset\Gamma^{(k)}
then
Q(k)^{-1}
composed with the projection on
\Gamma/\Gamma_{s}

contracts exponentially and this allows to prove the convergence.
In
[
44
]
and
[
20
]
, though, the mean value projection
\mathcal{M}^{(k)}
, obtained taking mean values of the function over every exchanged interval (see (
6.5
) below) takes values also outside
H(\pi^{(k)})
. Therefore, the contraction argument does not apply. To circumvent this problem, in
[
20
]
we have used the the projection on
\Gamma/\Gamma_{cs}
, where
\Gamma_{cs}
is the
central stable
space. Unfortunately, though, this is not sufficient now, when we consider almost every IET.

One of the novelties in this part of the article in relations to the previous correction operators constructions is that we consider

initial corrections

\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(k)}

obtained by composing
\mathcal{M}^{(k)}
with the projection
p_{H(\pi^{(k)})}
onto the space
H(\pi^{(k)})
(see §
6.2
). In view of the boundary operator estimate given by Lemma
3.4
(see §
3.1.10
), we can control the displacement
between
\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(k)}
and
\mathcal{M}^{(k)}
in terms of the boundary operator
\partial_{\pi^{(k)}}
(see §
6.2
, in particular the proof of Lemma
6.4
). It is
starting from this modified preliminary correction operators in step
(1)
that allows to prove convergence and hence leads to a good definition of correction (and correcting) operators in the more general setting of this paper, but also requires
proving a series of new inequalities and adding some new technical steps to the construction.
The
is devised exactly in order to guarantee convergence of this series.
In fact, to show that the series that gives
\Delta^{(k)}
(which is written in (
6.20
)) converges,
we will exploit the exponential contraction provided by the condition (
UDC1
) and (
3.6
).

The final part of the proof is to show that any correction operator
\mathfrak{h}
defined choosing a representative
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi)
for the equivalence class
\mathfrak{h}^{U}(\varphi)
in
H(\pi)/\Gamma^{s}
is such that
\|S(k)(\varphi-\mathfrak{h}(\varphi))\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}/|I^{(k)}|
has sub-exponential growth. This part follows quite closely the proof that we gave in
[
20
]
, along the lines of
[
44
]
.

6.2.
Preliminary corrections.

To define initial corrections, let us consider the linear operators on
{\operatorname{LG}}^{\operatorname{BV}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
, obtained by considering mean value-projections (which we defined in §
4.4
)

(6.5)

\mathcal{M}^{(k)}:{\operatorname{LG}}^{\operatorname{BV}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})\to\Gamma^{(k)},\quad\mathcal{M}^{(k)}\varphi=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}m(\varphi,I^{(k)}_{\alpha})\chi_{I^{(k)}_{\alpha}}.

6.2.1.
Initial corrections

The sequence of initial corrections that we want to use is given by composing these mean value-projections with the projection onto the space
H(\pi^{(k)})
. Recall that
p_{H(\pi^{(k)})}:\Gamma^{(k)}\to H(\pi^{(k)})
is the orthogonal projection
on
H(\pi^{(k)})
.

Definition 8

(Initial corrections)
.

Consider the operator

\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(k)}:{\operatorname{LG}}^{\operatorname{BV}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})\to H(\pi^{(k)}),\qquad\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(k)}:=p_{H(\pi^{(k)})}\circ{\mathcal{M}}^{(k)}.

Set the corresponding initial approximation of the correction operator to be

\displaystyle P^{(k)}_{0}:\quad

\displaystyle{\operatorname{LG}}^{\operatorname{BV}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})\to{\operatorname{LG}}^{\operatorname{BV}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\T1\i\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})

\displaystyle\varphi\mapsto P^{(k)}_{0}\varphi:=\varphi-\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(k)}\varphi.

The following properties of the initial corrections follow almost directly from the estimates on mean average corrections that we proved in §
4.4
as preparatory work, combined with the control of the projection through the boundary operator (given by Lemma
3.4
).

Lemma 6.4

(Initial correction estimates)
.

There exists a positive constant
C
such that for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
, for every
\varphi\in\operatorname{LG}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})
,

(6.6)

\displaystyle\|P_{0}^{(k)}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}

\displaystyle\leq C|I^{(k)}|\big(\log\|Q(k)\|\mathcal{AS}(\varphi)+\mathcal{LV}(\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi^{(k)}}(\varphi)\|\big)

(6.7)

\displaystyle\|P_{0}^{(k)}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}

\displaystyle\leq 4dC|I^{(k)}|\log\big(2\kappa\|Q(k)\|\big)\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{LV}}

(6.8)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(k)}\varphi\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa\sqrt{d}}{|I^{(k)}|}\|\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}.

Proof.

To estimate
P_{0}^{(k)}
, we will compare
\mathcal{M}^{(k)}
with
{\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(k)}}
, namely estimate

(6.9)

\displaystyle\|P_{0}^{(k)}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}

\displaystyle=\|\varphi-{\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(k)}}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}\leq\|\varphi-{\mathcal{M}}^{(k)}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}+\|{\mathcal{M}}^{(k)}\varphi-\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(k)}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}

Let us estimate separately the two terms in (
6.9
), namely the mean-value correcting operator and the difference of the mean value projections.

Estimating the mean-value correcting operator.

By the construction of the mean projection operator (see (
6.5
) and the definition of
m
in §
4.3
), we have

(6.10)

\|\mathcal{M}^{(k)}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}|m(\varphi,I_{\alpha}^{(k)})||I_{\alpha}^{(k)}|=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}|\int_{I_{\alpha}^{(k)}}\varphi(x)\,dx|\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}

and, by (
4.8
), we can therefore estimate the first term in (
6.9
) by

(6.11)

\|\varphi-\mathcal{M}^{(k)}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}\leq 8|I^{(k)}|\mathcal{LV}(\varphi).

Estimating the difference of the mean value projections.
To estimate the second term in (
6.9
), we recall that
p_{H(\pi^{(k)})}
, by Lemma
3.4
, satisfies
\|h-p_{H(\pi^{(k)})}h\|\leq C_{\mathcal{G}}\|\partial_{\pi^{(k)}}h\|

for every
k\geq 0
and
h\in\Gamma^{(k)}
. Thus,

(6.12)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|\mathcal{M}^{(k)}\varphi-p_{H(\pi^{(k)})}\mathcal{M}^{(k)}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}}&\leq{|I^{(k)}|}\ \|\mathcal{M}^{(k)}\varphi-p_{H(\pi^{(k)})}\mathcal{M}^{(k)}\varphi\|\leq C_{\mathcal{G}}{|I^{(k)}|}\ \|\partial_{\pi^{(k)}}\mathcal{M}^{(k)}\varphi\|.\end{split}

Moreover, by Proposition
4.6
,

(6.13)

\|\partial_{\pi^{(k)}}\mathcal{M}^{(k)}\varphi\|\leq\|\partial_{\pi^{(k)}}(\varphi)\|+\Big(1+\log\frac{2\kappa}{|I^{(k)}|}\Big)\mathcal{AS}(\varphi)+14d\kappa\mathcal{LV}(\varphi).

Proof of (
6.6
).
Going back to (
6.9
) and combining the two separate estimates just proved, namely
(
6.11
), (
6.12
) and (
6.13
), it follows that

\displaystyle\|P_{0}^{(k)}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}\leq|I^{(k)}|\Big(C_{\mathcal{G}}\|\partial_{\pi^{(k)}}(\varphi)\|+C_{\mathcal{G}}\Big(1+\log\frac{2\kappa}{|I^{(k)}|}\Big)\mathcal{AS}(\varphi)+(14d\kappa C_{\mathcal{G}}+8)\mathcal{LV}(\varphi)\Big).

As, by (
3.5
), we have
|I^{(k)}|^{-1}\leq\|Q(k)\|
, so we get
1+\log(2\kappa/|I^{(k)}|)=O(\|Q(k)\|)

which yields (
6.6
).

Proof of (
6.7
).

Recall now that, by the estimate (
4.18
) of
\|\partial_{\pi^{(k)}}(\varphi)\|
and balance, we have that
\|\partial_{\pi^{(k)}}(\varphi)\|\leq 2d\log(2\kappa/|I^{(k)}|)\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{LV}}\leq 2d\log(2\kappa d\|Q(k)\|)\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{LV}}
. Thus, as
\mathcal{AS}(\varphi)\leq\mathcal{LV}(\varphi)\leq\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{LV}}
,
it follows from (
6.6
) that

\displaystyle\|P_{0}^{(k)}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}

\displaystyle\leq C|I^{(k)}|\left(\log\|Q(k)\|+1+2d\log(2\kappa\|Q(k)\|)\right)\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{LV}}

\displaystyle\leq 4dC|I^{(k)}|\log(2\kappa\|Q(k)\|)\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{LV}}.

This proves also (
6.7
).

Proof of (
6.8
).

Finally, to prove (
6.8
), let us apply once more Lemma
3.4
,
which also gives that, for every
k\geq 0
and
h\in\Gamma^{(k)}
,
\|p_{H(\pi^{(k)})}h\|\leq\sqrt{d}\|h\|
.
Using this combined with (
6.10
), we get

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(k)}\varphi\|=\|p_{H(\pi^{(k)})}\mathcal{M}^{(k)}\varphi\|\leq\sqrt{d}\|\mathcal{M}^{(k)}\varphi\|\leq\frac{\kappa\sqrt{d}}{|I^{(k)}|}\|\mathcal{M}^{(k)}\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}\leq\frac{\kappa\sqrt{d}}{|I^{(k)}|}\|\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}

which proves also (
6.8
) and concludes the proof.
∎

6.2.2.
The series bringing back the corrections

We can now build the modification
\Delta^{(k)}
as a series (see (
6.15
) below), obtained by quotienting and pulling back the preliminary corrections defined in the previous section.

Consider, for
k\in\mathbb{N}
, the projections
U^{(k)}
on the quotient by the stable space, namely

U^{(k)}:{\operatorname{LG}}^{\operatorname{BV}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})\to{\operatorname{LG}}^{\operatorname{BV}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}.

Since
S(k,l)\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}=\Gamma^{(l)}_{s}
and
S(k,l):\Gamma^{(k)}\to\Gamma^{(l)}
is invertible,
the quotient linear transformation

S_{\flat}(k,l):{\operatorname{LG}}^{\operatorname{BV}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}\to{\operatorname{LG}}^{\operatorname{BV}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(l)}_{\alpha})/\Gamma^{(l)}_{s}

is well defined and

S_{\flat}(k,l):\Gamma^{(k)}/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}\to\Gamma^{(l)}/\Gamma^{(l)}_{s}

is invertible. Moreover,

(6.14)

S_{\flat}(k,l)\circ U^{(k)}\varphi=U^{(l)}\circ S(k,l)\varphi\text{
for }\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}^{\operatorname{BV}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha}).

The following Lemma shows that our Diophantine Condition guarantees the convergence of the series (
6.15
) obtained bringing back the corrections and hence it can be used to define a modification operator
\Delta^{(k)}
. Furthermore, it provides estimates that show that the modification operator is bounded.

Lemma 6.5

(Convergence of the modification series)
.

Suppose that
T
satisfies the
.
For every function
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})
, the following limit

(6.15)

\Delta^{(k)}\varphi=\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}U^{(k)}\circ S(k,l)^{-1}\circ\left(S(k,l)\circ P^{(k)}_{0}-P_{0}^{(l)}\circ S(k,l)\right)\varphi

exists in

H(\pi^{(k)})/\Gamma_{s}^{(k)}
and

(6.16)

\|\Delta^{(k)}\varphi\|\leq CK^{\prime}_{k}(\mathcal{LV}(\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi^{(k)}}(\varphi)\|).

Moreover,

(6.17)

\|\Delta^{(k)}(S(k)\varphi)\|\leq CK^{\prime}_{k}(\mathcal{LV}(\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi}(\varphi)\|)\text{ for every }\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha}).

If additionally
T
satisfies the
and
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
with
\mathcal{AS}(\varphi)=0
then for every
k\geq 1
we have

(6.18)

\|\Delta^{(k)}(S(k)\varphi)\|\leq CK_{k}(\mathcal{LV}(\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi}(\varphi)\|).

Let us first show that the Lemma implies that
\Delta^{(k)}
is bounded.

Corollary 6.6

(Boundedness of the modification)
.

For every
k\geq\mathbb{N}
, the operator
\Delta^{(k)}:{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})\to H(\pi^{(k)})/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}
defined by (
6.15
) is bounded.

Proof.

In view of (
6.16
) and (
4.18
), for every
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})
we have

(6.19)

\|\Delta^{(k)}\varphi\|\leq K^{\prime}_{k}(1+2d\log(2\kappa d\|Q(k)\|))\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{LV}}.

This shows that
\Delta^{(k)}:{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})\to H(\pi^{(k)})/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}
is bounded.
∎

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of Lemma
6.5
.

Proof of Lemma
6.5
.

Exploiting the telescopic nature of the series,
calculations similar to those in
[
20
]
show that

\displaystyle U^{(k)}

\displaystyle\circ S(k,l)^{-1}\circ\left(S(k,l)\circ P^{(k)}_{0}-P_{0}^{(l)}\circ S(k,l)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\leq r<l}(S_{\flat}(k,r+1))^{-1}\circ U^{(r+1)}\circ{\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(r+1)}}\circ S(r,r+1)\circ P_{0}^{(r)}\circ S(k,r).

It follows that we need to prove the convergence of the series

(6.20)

\sum_{r\geq k}(S_{\flat}(k,r+1))^{-1}\circ U^{(r+1)}\circ\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(r+1)}\circ S(r,r+1)\circ P_{0}^{(r)}\circ S(k,r)\varphi

in
H(\pi^{(k)})/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}
.

Convergence of the series and the estimate (
6.16
).
For any
r\geq k
, using (
6.8
), (
5.2
), (
6.6
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(r+1)}\circ S(r,r+1)\circ P_{0}^{(r)}\circ S(k,r)\varphi\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\kappa\sqrt{d}}{|I^{(r+1)}|}\|S(r,r+1)\circ P_{0}^{(r)}\circ S(k,r)\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(r+1)})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\kappa\sqrt{d}}{|I^{(r+1)}|}\|P_{0}^{(r)}\circ S(k,r)\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(r)})}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{|I^{(r)}|}{|I^{(r+1)}|}\big(\mathcal{AS}(S(k,r)\varphi)\log\|Q(r)\|+\mathcal{LV}(S(k,r)\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi^{(r)}}(S(k,r)\varphi)\|\big).

By the invariance of
\mathcal{AS}
,
\mathcal{LV}
and the boundary operator (see (
5.15
), (
5.6
) in Corollary
5.3
, (
5.7
)), (
3.5
) and (
4.5
) consecutively, we have

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(r+1)}\circ S(r,r+1)\circ P_{0}^{(r)}\circ S(k,r)\varphi\|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{|I^{(r)}|}{|I^{(r+1)}|}(\mathcal{LV}(S(k,r)\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi^{(r)}}(S(k,r)\varphi)\|+\mathcal{AS}(S(k,r)\varphi)\log\|Q(r)\|)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{|I^{(r)}|}{|I^{(r+1)}|}(\kappa(3+\log\|Q(k,r)\|)\mathcal{LV}(\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi^{(k)}}(\varphi)\|+\mathcal{AS}(\varphi)\log\|Q(r)\|)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\|Z(r+1)\|(\mathcal{LV}(\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi^{(k)}}(\varphi)\|)\log\|Q(r)\|.

In view of (
3.6
), for
0\leq k<l
and
h\in H(\pi^{(l)})

we have

(6.21)

\|(S_{\flat}(k,l))^{-1}\circ U^{(l)}(h)\|\leq\|Q_{s}(k,l)\|\|U^{(l)}(h)\|\leq\|Q_{s}(k,l)\|\|h\|.

Since
{\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(r+1)}}\circ S(r,r+1)\circ P_{0}^{(r)}\circ S(k,r)\varphi\in H(\pi^{(r+1)})
,
by (
6.21
),
the norm of the
r
-th element of the series (
6.20
)
is bounded from above by

C^{\prime}\|Q_{s}(k,r+1)\|\|Z(r+1)\|(\mathcal{LV}(\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi^{(k)}}(\varphi)\|)\log\|Q(r)\|.

Since
T
satisfies the
, by Proposition
3.9
, the series

\sum_{r\geq k}\|Q_{s}(k,r+1)\|\|Z(r+1)\|\log\|Q(r)\|

is convergent and its sum is
K^{\prime}_{k}
. As
\Delta^{(k)}\varphi
is the sum of the series (
6.20
),
it follows that the operator
\Delta^{(k)}
is well defined and (
6.16
) holds.

The estimates (
6.17
).

If
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
then we can repeat the above arguments for
S(k)\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})
instead of
\varphi
. As

\displaystyle\mathcal{LV}(S(k,r)(S(k)\varphi))\leq C\log\|Q(r)\|\mathcal{LV}(\varphi),

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\pi^{(r)}}(S(k,r)(S(k)\varphi))\|=\|\partial_{\pi}(\varphi)\|,\ \mathcal{AS}(S(k,r)(S(k)\varphi))=\mathcal{AS}(\varphi),

now the norm of the
r
-th element of the series (
6.20
) where
\varphi
is replaced by
S(k)\varphi
,
is bounded from above by

C^{\prime}\|Q_{s}(k,r+1)\|\|Z(r+1)\|(\mathcal{LV}(\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi}(\varphi)\|)\log\|Q(r)\|.

This gives also (
6.17
).

Symmetric singularities estimates.
Now suppose that
T
satisfies the
and
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
with
\mathcal{AS}(\varphi)=0
. Using (
5.12
) and reasoning similar to the above
we obtain

\displaystyle\|P_{0}^{(r)}\circ S(k,r)(S(k)\varphi)\|_{L^{1}(I^{(r)})}

\displaystyle=\|P_{0}^{(r)}(S(r)\varphi)\|_{L^{1}(I^{(r)})}\leq C|I^{(r)}|(\mathcal{LV}(S(r)\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi^{(r)}}(S(r)\varphi)\|)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\|Z(r+1)\||I^{(r+1)}|(\mathcal{LV}(\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi}(\varphi)\|).

Thus

\displaystyle\|(S_{\flat}(k,r+1))^{-1}\circ U^{(r+1)}\circ{\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(r+1)}}\circ S(r,r+1)\circ P_{0}^{(r)}\circ S(k,r)(S(k)\varphi)\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|Q_{s}(k,r+1)\|\|Z(r+1)\|(\mathcal{LV}(\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi}(\varphi)\|).

This gives (
6.18
).
∎

6.2.3.
The equivariant correction operators

Consider now the operator

P^{(k)}:{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})\to{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}

given by
P^{(k)}=U^{(k)}\circ P_{0}^{(k)}-\Delta^{(k)}
. As the operators
U^{(k)}
and
P_{0}^{(k)}
(see (
6.7
)) are bounded
linear operators,
P^{(k)}
is also linear and bounded when
{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}
is equipped with the
L^{1}(I^{(k)})/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}
norm.
We will now show that this modified correcting operator satisfies the sough equivariance property, i.e.
commutes
with the operation of considering special Birkhoff sums.

Lemma 6.7

(Equivariance)
.

Suppose that
T
satisfies the
. For all
0\leq k\leq l
we have

(6.22)

S_{\flat}(k,l)\circ P^{(k)}=P^{(l)}\circ S(k,l).

Moreover, for every
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
we have

(6.23)

\frac{1}{|I^{(k)}|}\|P^{(k)}(S(k)\varphi)\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}}\leq\Theta_{k}(\varphi):=CK^{\prime}_{k}\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{LV}}.

If additionally
T
satisfies the
and
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
with
\mathcal{AS}(\varphi)=0
then (
6.23
) holds with

\Theta_{k}(\varphi):=CK_{k}(\mathcal{LV}(\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi}(\varphi)\|).

Proof.

The condition (
6.22
) is a direct consequence of the definition of

P^{(k)}
. Its proof run along similar lines as the proof of the first part of Lemma 4.2 in
[
20
]
.

In view of
\|{U^{(k)}}\|=1
,
(
6.6
), (
6.17
), (
5.14
) and (
5.7
) we get

\displaystyle\|P^{(k)}(S(k)\varphi)\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}}

\displaystyle\leq\|P_{0}^{(k)}(S(k)\varphi)\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}+|I^{(k)}|\|\Delta^{(k)}(S(k)\varphi)\|\leq C|I^{(k)}|K^{\prime}_{k}(\mathcal{LV}(\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi}(\varphi)\|).

Moreover, using (
6.7
) and (
6.19
) instead of (
6.6
) and (
6.17
), we also have

\displaystyle\|P^{(k)}(S(k)\varphi)\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}}\leq C|I^{(k)}|K^{\prime}_{k}\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{LV}},

which give (
6.23
).

Symmetric singularities case.
Suppose that
T
satisfies the
and
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
with
\mathcal{AS}(\varphi)=0
. Then, using (
6.18
) and (
5.12
) instead of (
6.16
) and (
5.14
),
we get (
6.23
) with
\Theta_{k}(\varphi)=CK_{k}(\mathcal{LV}(\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi}(\varphi)\|)
.
∎

6.3.
Proof of Theorem
6.1

Now that we have build the correcting operator
P^{(0)}
with values in the space
{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(0)}_{\alpha})/\Gamma^{(0)}_{s}
and the desired equivariance properties (see Lemma
6.7
, we want to
check that any choice of representative for the equivalence class
P^{(0)}\varphi
satisfies the desired growth estimates and then
to lift
P^{(0)}
to an operator
I-\mathfrak{h}
with values in
{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})
. We first prove a Lemma that shows that any choice of representative of the equivalence class
P^{(0)}(\varphi)
satisfies the desired estimates hold (see Lemma
6.8
and in particular the estimates in
(ii)
) and then use it to show that the correction is uniquely defined (see Corollary
6.9
). The proof of Theorem
6.1
then follows easily from this Lemma
6.8
and Corollary
6.9
and is given at the end of the section.

Recall that we defined the equivariant correction operator
P^{(0)}
by setting
P^{(0)}=U^{(0)}\circ P_{0}^{(0)}-\Delta^{(0)}
.
We say that a map
\widehat{\varphi}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(0)}_{\alpha})
is a
correction
of
\varphi
if it is a
representative of the corrected equivalence class

P^{(0)}\varphi
, i.e.
U^{(0)}(\widehat{\varphi})=P^{(0)}(\varphi)
.
With this in mind, the following Lemma shows that any correction of
\varphi

satisfies the desired estimates on the growth of Birkhoff sums. The constants
C_{k}
and
C^{\prime}_{k}
which appear in the estimates of Birkhoff sums of corrected functions (see part
(ii)
of the Lemma below) are given by the
Diophantine series

C_{k}(T)
and
C^{\prime}_{k}(T)

which we defined for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
in §
4
and showed that they converge and hence are well defined under the assumption that
T
satisfies the
or
.

Lemma 6.8

(Birkhoff sums estimates for corrected functions)
.

Suppose that
T
satisfies the
.
Assume that
\varphi,\widehat{\varphi}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(0)}_{\alpha})
and that

U^{(0)}\widehat{\varphi}=P^{(0)}\varphi
. Then:

(i)

\widehat{\varphi}-\varphi\in H(\pi^{(0)})
.

(ii)

For any
k\geq 1
we have

(6.24)

\frac{\|S(k)(\widehat{\varphi})\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}}{|I^{(k)}|}\leq C\Big(C^{\prime}_{k}\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{LV}}+C^{\prime\prime}_{k}\frac{\|{\widehat{\varphi}}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(0)})}}{|I^{(0)}|}\Big)

and if
T
satisfies the
and
\mathcal{AS}(\varphi)=0
then

(6.25)

\frac{\|S(k)(\widehat{\varphi})\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}}{|I^{(k)}|}\leq C\Big(C_{k}\big(\mathcal{LV}(\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi^{(0)}}(\varphi)\|\big)+C^{\prime\prime}_{k}\frac{\|\widehat{\varphi}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(0)})}}{|I^{(0)}|}\Big)

with
C_{k}:=C_{k}(T)
,
C^{\prime}_{k}:=C^{\prime}_{k}(T)
(refer to §
4
for the definition of the Diophantine series
C_{k}
and
C^{\prime}_{k}
) and
C^{\prime\prime}_{k}:=\|Q_{s}(k)\|
.

The Lemma shows that every correction of
\varphi
is of the form
\varphi-h
with
h\in H(\pi^{(0)})
. Let us first show that the Lemma also implies that the correction
h
is uniquely defined, once we fix a complement to
\Gamma_{s}^{(0)}
in
H(\pi^{(0)})
.

Corollary 6.9

(Uniqueness of the correction)
.

Fix a subspace
F\subset H(\pi^{(0)})
such that
F\oplus\Gamma_{s}^{(0)}=H(\pi^{(0)})
. Suppose that
h_{1},h_{2}\in F
are two vectors such
that

U^{(0)}(\varphi-h_{1})=U^{(0)}(\varphi-h_{2})=P^{(0)}\varphi.

Then
h_{1}=h_{2}
.

Proof.

In view of (
6.24
) of Lemma
6.8
combined with (
3.28
), we have

\limsup_{k\to+\infty}\frac{\log\frac{\|S(k)(\varphi-h_{i})\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}}{|I^{(k)}|}}{k}\leq 0\text{ for }i=1,2.

Thus

\limsup_{k\to+\infty}\frac{\log\|Q(k)(h_{1}-h_{2})\|}{k}\leq 0.

As
h_{1}-h_{2}\in H(\pi^{(0)})
, by the condition (
O
) in Definition
3
, it follows that
h_{1}-h_{2}\in\Gamma^{(0)}_{s}
.
Since
h_{1}-h_{2}\in F
and
\Gamma^{(0)}_{s}\cap F=\{{0}\}
, we have
h_{1}=h_{2}
.
∎

Let us now prove the Lemma.

Proof of Lemma
6.8
.

Since by definition of the operators

U^{(0)}\widehat{\varphi}=P^{(0)}\varphi=U^{(0)}\circ P_{0}^{(0)}\varphi-\Delta^{(0)}\varphi=U^{(0)}\varphi-U^{(0)}\circ\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(0)}\varphi-\Delta^{(0)}\varphi,

we have

U^{(0)}(\varphi-\widehat{\varphi})=U^{(0)}\circ\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(0)}\varphi+\Delta^{(0)}\varphi\in H(\pi^{(0)})/\Gamma_{s}^{(0)}.

Therefore

(6.26)

\varphi-\widehat{\varphi}\in H(\pi^{(0)})+\Gamma_{s}^{(0)}\subset H(\pi^{(0)}).

In view of (
6.14
) and (
6.22
),

U^{(k)}\circ S(k)\widehat{\varphi}=S_{\flat}(k)\circ U^{(0)}\widehat{\varphi}=S_{\flat}(k)\circ P^{(0)}\varphi=P^{(k)}\circ S(k)\varphi.

Therefore,
from (
6.23
), we have

\|U^{(k)}\circ S(k)\widehat{\varphi}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}}=\|P^{(k)}(S(k)\varphi)\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}}|I^{(k)}|\leq\Theta_{k}(\varphi).

It follows from the definition of
\|\cdot\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}}
on the quotient space that
for every
k\geq 0
there exists

\varphi_{k}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})
and
s_{k}\in\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}
such that

(6.27)

S(k)\widehat{\varphi}=\varphi_{k}+s_{k}\text{ and
}\frac{\|\varphi_{k}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}}{|I^{(k)}|}\leq\Theta_{k}(\varphi).

Next note that

(6.28)

\varphi_{k+1}+s_{k+1}=S(k+1)\widehat{\varphi}=S(k,k+1)S(k)\widehat{\varphi}=S(k,k+1)\varphi_{k}+Q(k,k+1)s_{k},

so setting
\Delta s_{k+1}=s_{k+1}-Z(k+1)s_{k}
(
\Delta s_{0}=s_{0}
) we
have

\Delta s_{k+1}=-\varphi_{k+1}+S(k,k+1)\varphi_{k}.

Moreover, by (
5.2
), for
k\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|\Delta s_{k+1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa}{|I^{(k+1)}|}\|\Delta s_{k+1}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k+1)})}=\frac{\kappa}{|I^{(k+1)}|}\|\varphi_{k+1}-S(k,k+1)\varphi_{k}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k+1)})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa}{|I^{(k+1)}|}\big(\|\varphi_{k+1}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k+1)})}+\|S(k,k+1)\varphi_{k}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k+1)})}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\Big(\frac{\|\varphi_{k+1}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k+1)})}}{|I^{(k+1)}|}+\frac{|I^{(k)}|}{|I^{(k+1)}|}\frac{\|\varphi_{k}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}}{|I^{(k)}|}\Big).

Next, by (
6.27
) and (
3.5
), it follows that

\|\Delta s_{k+1}\|\leq\kappa\big(\|Z(k+1)\|\Theta_{k}(\varphi)+\Theta_{k+1}(\varphi)\big)\text{ for }k\geq 0

and

\displaystyle\|\Delta s_{0}\|\leq\kappa\frac{\|s_{0}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(0)})}}{|I^{(0)}|}=\kappa\frac{\|\widehat{\varphi}-\varphi_{0}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(0)})}}{|I^{(0)}|}\leq\kappa\frac{\|\widehat{\varphi}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(0)})}}{|I^{(0)}|}+\kappa\Theta_{0}(\varphi).

Since
s_{k}=\sum_{0\leq l\leq k}Q(l,k)\Delta s_{l}
and
\Delta s_{l}\in\Gamma^{(l)}_{s}
, setting
\Theta_{-1}:=0
, we have

\displaystyle\|s_{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{0\leq l\leq k}\|Q(l,k)\Delta s_{l}\|\leq\sum_{0\leq l\leq k}\|Q_{s}(l,k)\|\|\Delta s_{l}\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\sum_{0\leq l\leq k}\|Q_{s}(l,k)\|(\Theta_{l}(\varphi)+\|Z(l)\|\Theta_{l-1}(\varphi))+\kappa\|Q_{s}(k)\|\frac{\|\widehat{\varphi}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(0)})}}{|I^{(0)}|}.

In view of
(
6.27
) and taking
\Theta_{k}(\varphi)=CK^{\prime}_{k}\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{LV}}
, it follows that for
k\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\frac{\|S(k)\widehat{\varphi}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}}{|I^{(k)}|}\leq\frac{\|{\varphi}_{k}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}}{|I^{(k)}|}+\|s_{k}\|\leq d\kappa C\Big(C^{\prime}_{k}\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{LV}}+C^{\prime\prime}_{k}\frac{\|{\varphi}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(0)})}}{|I^{(0)}|}\Big).

If
T
satisfies the
and
\mathcal{AS}(\varphi)=0
then the same argument applied to
\Theta_{k}(\varphi)=CK_{k}(\mathcal{LV}(\varphi)+\|\partial_{\pi^{(0)}}(\varphi)\|)
shows also
(
6.25
).
∎

We have now all the elements to conclude the proof of Theorem
6.1
.

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

Fix a subspace
F\subset H(\pi^{(0)})
such that
F\oplus\Gamma_{s}^{(0)}=H(\pi^{(0)})
. Choose any
\widehat{\varphi}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
with
U^{(0)}(\widehat{\varphi})=P^{(0)}\varphi
. By
(i)
of Lemma
6.8
,

\widehat{\varphi}-\varphi\in H(\pi^{(0)})
. Therefore,
there exist
h\in F
and

h^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{(0)}_{s}
such that

\varphi-h=\widehat{\varphi}+h^{\prime}
. As
U^{(0)}(\widehat{\varphi})=P^{(0)}\varphi
, it follows that

U^{(0)}(\varphi-h)=U^{(0)}(\widehat{\varphi}+h^{\prime})=U^{(0)}(\widehat{\varphi})=P^{(0)}\varphi.

By Corollary
6.9
,
for every

\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
there exists a unique
h=h(\varphi)\in F
such that
U^{(0)}(\varphi-h)=P^{(0)}\varphi
.
Thus, there
exists a unique linear operator

\mathfrak{h}:{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})\to F
(the

correction operator
) such that

(6.29)

U^{(0)}(\varphi-\mathfrak{h}(\varphi))=P^{(0)}(\varphi).

As the operator

P^{(0)}:{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})\to{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})/\Gamma^{(0)}_{s}

is bounded, by the
closed graph theorem, the operator
{\mathfrak{h}}
is also
bounded. Indeed, if
\varphi_{n}\to\varphi
in
{{\operatorname{LG}}}

and
{\mathfrak{h}}(\varphi_{n})\to h
in

F
then have both

\displaystyle{P}^{(0)}\varphi_{n}\to{P}^{(0)}\varphi=U^{(0)}(\varphi-{\mathfrak{h}}(\varphi)),

\displaystyle{P}^{(0)}\varphi_{n}=U^{(0)}(\varphi_{n}-{\mathfrak{h}}(\varphi_{n}))\to U^{(0)}(\varphi-h).

It follows that
{\mathfrak{h}}(\varphi)-h\in F
and at the same time

{\mathfrak{h}}(\varphi)-h\in\Gamma^{(0)}_{s}
, so

h={\mathfrak{h}}(\varphi)
. Since the vector norm and the

L^{1}
-norm are equivalent on
\Gamma^{(0)}
,
we get that the operator is bounded.

Suppose now that

\mathfrak{h}(\varphi)=0
. Then

U^{(0)}(\varphi)=U^{(0)}(\varphi-\mathfrak{h}(\varphi))=P^{(0)}(\varphi).

Therefore, (
6.3
) and (
6.4
) follow directly from
(
6.24
) and (
6.25
) of the part
(ii)
of Lemma
6.8
respectively. This concludes the proof and proves as well the statement of Remark
6.3
.
∎

7.
Deviations of Birkhoff sums and integrals

In this section we prove the main results on the deviation spectrum of locally Hamiltonian flows, by first reducing the study of integrals along a locally Hamiltonian flow to the study of Birkhoff sums (see §
7.1
), then exploiting the correction operator built in §
6
to build (in the spirit of Bufetov functionals and Bufetov work
[
6
]
) the cocycles which correspond to pure power behaviour, see §
7.2
.

7.1.
Estimates of Birkhoff integrals through Birkhoff sums

In this section we provide effective estimate for the growth of Birkhoff integrals (Proposition
7.7
),
which can be applied when the roof function
g
is unbounded. We first exploit the special flow representation of the flow as a suspension flow over an IET under a roof function with logarithmic singularities (refer to §
2.3
) to reduce to estimates of Birkhoff sums, see §
7.1.1
. We then exploit a standard decomposition of Birkhoff sums in special Birkhoff sums, see §
7.1.2
. The estimates relies on the speed of decay of the tails of
g
. This crucial new ingredient is explained in §
7.1.3
. The main result of this section is then the estimate given by Proposition
7.7
in §
7.1.4
.

7.1.1.
Reduction of integrals along the flow to Birkhoff sums

Let
T:I\to I
be an ergodic IET and let
g:I\to{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\cup\{+\infty\}
be an integrable function such that
\underline{g}=\inf_{x\in I}g(x)>0
. Following §
2.2.2
, we denote by

T^{g}_{\mathbb{R}}:I^{g}\to I^{g}
the special flow over
T
under the roof
g
.
For every integrable function
f:I^{g}\to{\mathbb{R}}
let
\varphi_{f}:I\to{\mathbb{R}}
be given by
\varphi_{f}(x)=\int_{0}^{g(x)}f(x,r)\,dr
.
By Fubini’s theorem,
\varphi_{f}
is well defined for a.e.
x\in I
, is integrable and

\int_{I}\varphi_{f}(x)\,dx=\int_{I^{g}}f(x,r)\,dx\,dr.

For every
(x,r)\in I^{g}
and
s>0
denote by
n(x,r,s)\geq 0
the number of times the orbit segment
\{T^{g}_{t}(x,r):t\in[0,s]\}
crosses the interval
I
(identified with
I\times\{0\}
), i.e. the unique non-negative integer number such that

(7.1)

g^{(n(x,r,s))}(x)\leq r+s<g^{(n(x,r,s)+1)}(x).

Then
0\leq n(x,r,s)\leq s/\underline{g}+1
.

For every
c\geq\underline{g}
, let
I_{c}\subset I
be the level set defined by
g(x)\leq c
for every
x\in I_{c}
. Moreover, for every
s\geq 0
let

(7.2)

A_{c}^{s}:=\{(x,r)\in I^{g}:x\in I_{c}\}\setminus\{T^{g}_{-t}(x,0):x\in I\setminus I_{c},\,0\leq t\leq s\}\subset I^{g}.

The following elementary Lemma relates the Birkhoff integrals of
f
for the flow
T^{g}_{\mathbb{R}}
with the Birkhoff sums of
\varphi_{f}
for the IET
T
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Suppose that
f:I^{g}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is bounded. For every
s>0
and
c\geq\underline{g}

if
(x,r)\in A^{s}_{c}
then

T^{i}x\in I_{c}
for all
0\leq i\leq n(x,r,s)
, and

(7.3)

\Big|\int_{0}^{s}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,r))\,dt\Big|\leq|\varphi_{f}^{(n(x,r,s))}(x)|+2c\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Proof.

For every
(x,r)\in A_{c}^{s}
we decompose the orbit segment
\{T^{g}_{t}(x,r):t\in[0,s]\}
into
n(x,r,s)+1
-pieces
using its meeting points with
I\times\{0\}\subset I^{g}
, i.e. along crossing times

0<t_{1}<\ldots<t_{n}<s,\text{ where }n:=n(x,r,s)\text{ and }t_{i}:=g^{(i)}(x)-r\text{ for }1\leq i\leq n.

Then
T^{g}_{t_{i}}(x,r)=(T^{i}x,0)
for
0\leq i\leq n
, with
t_{0}:=-r
. As
(x,r)\in A_{c}^{s}
, it follows that

g(T^{i}x)\leq c
for
0\leq i\leq n
, which proves the first part of the Lemma. As
t_{i+1}-t_{i}=g^{(i+1)}(x)-g^{(i)}(x)=g(T^{i}x)
, according to the decomposition we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,r))\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t_{0}}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,r))\,dt+\sum_{0\leq j<n}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}f(T_{t}^{g}(x,r))\,dt+\int_{t_{n}}^{s}f(T_{t}^{g}(x,r))\,dt

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j<n}\int_{0}^{g(T^{j}x)}f(T^{j}x,t)\,dt-\int_{0}^{r}f(x,t)\,dt+\int_{0}^{s-t_{n}}f(T^{n}x,t)\,dt

\displaystyle=\varphi_{f}^{(n)}(x)-\int_{0}^{r}f(x,t)\,dt+\int_{0}^{s-t_{n}}f(T^{n}x,t)\,dt.

Since
r<g(x)\leq c
and
s-t_{n}<g(T^{n})\leq c
, we also have

\Big|\int_{0}^{r}f(x,t)\,dt\Big|\leq\int_{0}^{g(x)}|f(x,t)|\,dt\leq c\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}

and

\Big|\int_{0}^{s-t_{n}}f(T^{n}x,t)\,dt\Big|\leq\int_{0}^{g(T^{n}x)}|f(T^{n}x,t)|\,dt\leq c\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Therefore

\Big|\int_{0}^{s}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,r))\,dt\Big|\leq\big|\varphi_{f}^{(n(x,r,s))}(x)\big|+2c\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}

for every
(x,r)\in A_{c}^{s}
.
∎

7.1.2.
Decomposition of Birkhoff sums in special Birkhoff sums

In this subsection we estimate
\varphi^{(n)}(x)
by decomposing the sum into special Birkhoff sums introduced by Zorich in
[
71
]
. Let
T:I\to I
be an arbitrary IET satisfying Keane’s condition.
For every
x\in I
and
n\geq 0
set

m(x,n)=m(x,n,T):=\max\big\{l\geq 0:\#\{0\leq k\leq n:T^{k}x\in I^{(l)}\}\geq 2\big\}.

Proposition 7.2

(see
[
71
]
or
[
67
]
)
.

For every
x\in I
and
n>0
we have

\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}Q_{\alpha}(m)\leq n\leq d\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}Q_{\alpha}(m+1)=d\|Q(m+1)\|,\text{ where }m=m(x,n).

Since the sequence
\big(\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}Q_{\alpha}(m)\big)_{m\geq 0}

increases to the infinity

m(n)=m(n,T):=\max\{m(x,n):x\in I\}

is well defined.
If
T
additionally satisfies the
then, by (
UDC3
) and (
3.18
), for every
\tau>0

we have

(7.4)

e^{\lambda_{1}m(n)}\leq O(\|Q(m(n))\|)\leq O\Big(\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}Q_{\alpha}(m(n))^{1+\tau}\Big)=O(n^{1+\tau}).

Proposition 7.3
.

For every
s>0
and
c\geq\underline{g}

if
(x,r)\in A_{c}^{s}
then

(7.5)

\big|\varphi_{f}^{(n(x,r,s))}(x)\big|\leq 2\sum_{k=0}^{m(n(x,r,s))}\|Z(k+1)\|\|S(k)\varphi_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(I^{(k)}(c))},

with

I^{(k)}(c):=\bigcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\{x\in I^{(k)}_{\alpha}:\forall_{0\leq j<Q_{\alpha}(k)}T^{j}x\in I_{c}\}.

Proof.

Fix
s>0
and
c>0
.
For each point
(x,r)\in I^{g}
we will decompose the orbit segment

x,Tx,\ldots,T^{n-1}x\text{ with }n:=n(x,r,s)

into segments. Let
m:=m(x,n)
, so
I^{(m)}
is hit by the the orbit segment at least twice
and
I^{(m+1)}
at most once. For each
0\leq k\leq m
let

n_{k}^{+}=\min\{j\geq 0:T^{j}x\in I^{(k)}\},\quad n_{k}^{-}=\min\{j\geq 1:T^{n-j}x\in I^{(k)}\}.

For
0\leq k<m
we also have

T^{n_{k+1}^{+}}x=(T^{(k)})^{b_{k}^{+}}T^{n_{k}^{+}}x\text{ and }T^{n-n_{k+1}^{-}}x=(T^{(k)})^{-b_{k}^{-}}T^{n-n_{k}}x

with

(7.6)

0\leq b_{k}^{+},b_{k}^{-}<\|Z(k+1)\|.

Moreover,

(7.7)

(T^{(m)})^{b_{m}}T^{n_{m}^{+}}x=T^{n-n_{m}^{-}}x\text{ with }1\leq b_{m}\leq\|Z(m+1)\|.

Here
T^{n_{m}^{+}}x
,
T^{n-n_{m}^{-}}x
are the first and the last visit of the orbit segment in
I^{(m)}
.
Thus

\displaystyle\varphi_{f}^{(n)}(x)=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\sum_{j=0}^{b_{k}^{+}-1}(S(k)\varphi_{f})((T^{(k)})^{j}T^{n_{k}^{+}}x)+\sum_{j=0}^{b_{m}-1}(S(m)\varphi_{f})((T^{(m)})^{j}T^{n_{m}^{+}}x)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\sum_{j=0}^{b_{k}^{-}-1}(S(k)\varphi_{f})((T^{(k)})^{j}T^{n-n_{k+1}^{-}}x).

If
(x,r)\in A_{s}^{c}
, then, by the first part of Lemma
7.1
,
T^{l}x\in I_{c}
for all
0\leq l\leq n
. Hence

(T^{(k)})^{j}T^{n_{k}^{+}}x,(T^{(k)})^{j}T^{n-n_{k+1}^{-}}x\in I^{(k)}(c).

In view of (
7.6
) and (
7.7
), it follows that

|\varphi_{f}^{(n)}(x)|\leq 2\sum_{k=0}^{m}\|Z(k+1)\|\|S(k)\varphi_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(I^{(k)}(c))},

which proves (
7.5
).
∎

7.1.3.
Control of the tail behaviour

Let
g:I\to{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\cup\{+\infty\}
be an integrable roof map with
\underline{g}=\min_{x\in I}g(x)>0
.
Suppose that for every
s\geq\underline{g}
we have a subset
I_{s}\subset I
such that
g(x)\leq s
for
x\in I_{s}
.
Let us consider the map
\xi:[\underline{g},+\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
given by

(7.8)

\xi(s):=Leb(I\setminus I_{s}).

Denote by
F_{g}:{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
the
tail distribution function
of
g
, i.e.

F_{g}(s):=Leb(\{x\in I:g(x)>s\})\text{ for }s\geq 0.

By definition,

(7.9)

\{x\in I:g(x)>s\}\subset I\setminus I_{s}\text{ and }F_{g}(s)\leq\xi(s)\text{ for }s\geq\underline{g}.

Lemma 7.4
.

Suppose that
\xi:[\underline{g},+\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
is decreasing integrable and of class
C^{1}
map
with
\lim_{s\to+\infty}s\xi(s)=0
. Let us consider
\Xi:[\underline{g},+\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
be given by
\Xi(s)=\int_{s}^{+\infty}\xi(t)\,dt
for
s\geq\underline{g}
. Then for every
s>0
and
c\geq\underline{g}
we have

(7.10)

Leb(I^{g}\setminus A_{c}^{s})\leq s\xi(c)+2c\xi(c)+\Xi(c).

Proof.

By the definition of
\Xi
and (
7.9
), using integration by part we have

\int_{\{x\in I:g(x)\geq c\}}g(x)dx=-\int_{c}^{\infty}t\,dF_{g}(t)=cF_{g}(c)+\int_{c}^{\infty}F_{g}(t)\,dt\leq c\xi(c)+\Xi(c).

Therefore

\int_{I\setminus I_{c}}g(x)dx\leq\int_{\{x\in I:g(x)\geq c\}}g(x)dx+\int_{\{x\in I\setminus I_{c}:g(x)\leq c\}}g(x)dx\leq 2c\xi(c)+\Xi(c).

It follows that for every
c\geq\underline{g}
and
s\geq 0
we have

Leb(I^{g}\setminus A_{c}^{s})\leq\int_{I\setminus I_{c}}g(x)dx+sLeb(I\setminus I_{c})=s\xi(c)+2c\xi(c)+\Xi(c),

which completes the proof
∎

Remark 7.5
.

Note that, by definition,
\Xi
is a decreasing
C^{2}
-map and
\displaystyle\lim_{s\to+\infty}\Xi(s)=0
.

Remark 7.6
.

Suppose that the roof function
g\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
. Then there exist two positive constants

C,b>0
such that for every
s\geq\underline{g}
we have

g(x)\leq s\text{ for all }x\in\bigcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}[l_{\alpha}+Ce^{-bs},r_{\alpha}-Ce^{-bs}].

Let us define the following sets (corresponding tail level sets):

I_{s}:=\bigcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}[l_{\alpha}+Ce^{-bs},r_{\alpha}-Ce^{-bs}]\text{ for any }s\geq\underline{g}.

Then
\xi(s)=dCe^{-bs}
and
\Xi(s)=(dC/b)e^{-bs}
,
so they satisfy the assumptions of Lemma
7.4
. In view of Lemma
7.4
, taking
c(s)=\tfrac{a}{b}\log s
for some
a>1
we have

(7.11)

Leb(I^{g}\setminus A_{c(s)}^{s})\leq sdCs^{-a}+2\frac{a}{b}(\log s)dCs^{-a}+\frac{dC}{b}s^{-a}=O(s^{-(a-1)}),

so the measure of
I^{g}\setminus A_{c(s)}^{s}
decays with the polynomial speed.

7.1.4.
Estimates of integrals and tails

We can now combine the results on the two previous subsections, i.e. the reduction of integrals along the flow to Birkhoff sums (Lemma
7.1
) and the decomposition of Birkhoff sums into special Birkhoff sums (Propostion
7.3
), to get the following estimate of ergodic integrals in terms of special Birkhoff sums:

Proposition 7.7
.

Let
\eta:{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}\to[\underline{g},+\infty)
be an increasing
C^{1}
-map.
Let
f:I^{g}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a measurable bounded map. Then, for every
s\in{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
,

(7.12)

Leb(I^{g}\setminus A^{s}_{\eta(s)})\leq s\ \xi(\eta(s))+2\eta(s)\xi(\eta(s))+\Xi(\eta(s))

(where
\xi(\,\cdot\,)
is defined by (
7.8
) and
\Xi(\,\cdot\,)
is given by Lemma
7.4
),
and for every
(x,r)\in A^{s}_{\eta(s)}
,

(7.13)

\Big|\int_{0}^{s}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,r))dt\Big|\leq 2\sum_{k\geq 0}\|Z(k+1)\|\|S(k)\varphi_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(I^{(k)}(\eta(s)))}+2\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\eta^{2}(s)

Proof.

The result follows by combining Lemma
7.1
, Proposition
7.3
and Lemma
7.4
with
c=\eta(s)
.
∎

7.2.
Deviation spectrum and asymptotic behaviour of ergodic integrals

We present in
this section the proof of Theorem
1.4
and the first part of Theorem
1.3
, namely the existence of the asymptotic spectrum for ergodic integrals both in the minimal and non-minimal case. We first define, in §
7.2.1
, the cocycles that will govern the asymptotic behaviour of the ergodic integrals. Notice that, since we are proving at the same time the existence of the expansions in Theorems
1.3
and
1.4
, we will define cocycles
u_{\sigma}
parametrized by
\sigma\in Fix(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})\cap M^{\prime}
also when considering the restriction of a typical
\psi_{\mathbb{R}}\in\mathcal{U}_{\neg min}
to a minimal component
M^{\prime}
(even if these do not appear explicitely in the statement of Theorem
1.4
, where they are absorbed in
err(f,T,\cdot)
). We then estimate the
error term
and shows that it exhibit subpolynomial deviations, see §
7.2.2
and then prove in §
7.2.3
that the cocycles that we build have the desired
pure power
behaviour, i.e. each has oscillations of the order of
T^{\nu_{i}}
where
\nu_{i}
is one of the
g
distinct exponents in the power spectrum. Finally, in §
7.2.4
we conclude the proof.

7.2.1.
Definition of the distributions and the cocycles.

Assume that
T=T_{(\pi,\lambda)}
satisfies the
. Then, in view of the Oseledets genericity property (
O
) of the
condition (refer to Definition
3
) there exists vectors

h_{1},\ldots,h_{g}\in H(\pi^{(0)})
such that

(7.14)

\lim_{k\to+\infty}\frac{1}{k}\|Q(k)h_{i}\|=\lambda_{i}\text{ for }1\leq i\leq g.

and furthermore
\operatorname{span}\{h_{1},\ldots,h_{g}\}\oplus\Gamma^{(0)}_{s}=H(\pi^{(0)})
.
We will now use these vectors
h_{i}
to define the distributions and the cocycles which appear in the asymptotic expansion.

The distributions.
By Theorem
6.1
(in view of Remark
6.3
) and Corollary
6.2

applied to
F:=\operatorname{span}\{h_{1},\ldots,h_{g}\}
,
there exists a bounded operator

\mathfrak{h}:{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})\to F
,
such that
\mathfrak{h}(h)=h
for every
h\in F
and for every
\tau>0
if
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
and
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi)=0
then

(7.15)

\frac{\|S(k)\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}}{|I^{(k)}|}=O(e^{\tau k}).

Let
d_{i}:{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})\to{\mathbb{R}}
,
i=1,\ldots,g
be bounded operators such that

(7.16)

\mathfrak{h}(\varphi)=\sum_{i=1}^{g}d_{i}(\varphi)h_{i}\text{ for every }\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha}).

We can then define bounded operators
D_{i}:C^{2+\epsilon}(M)\to{\mathbb{R}}
, for
i=1,\ldots,g
, by using the map
f\mapsto\varphi_{f}

(see Proposition
4.1
for its basic properties)
which associates to an observable
f:M\to{\mathbb{R}}
the cocycle which arise in the skew-product representation of the Poincaré map described in §
2.3.3
and
setting

(7.17)

D_{i}(f):=d_{i}(\varphi_{f}),\qquad 1\leq i\leq g.

We will prove in §
7.2.4
that these are the distributions which enter in the asymptotic expansion.

The power growth cocycles.
To construct the cocycles we exploit the following Lemma, proved in
[
10
]
.

Lemma 7.8

(Lemma 7.4 in
[
10
]
)
.

For every
h\in H(\pi)
there exists a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
-function
f:M\to{\mathbb{R}}
, which vanishes on a neighborhood
\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})
, such that
\varphi_{f}=h
.

Let
f_{i}\in C^{\infty}(M)
be the observable such that
\varphi_{f_{i}}=h_{i}
, given by Lemma
7.8
applied to
h=h_{i}
.
Let us now define

u_{i}(T,x):=\int_{0}^{T}f_{i}(\psi_{s}(x))\,ds,\qquad\text{for}\ 1\leq i\leq g.

The singular cocycles.

For every
\sigma\in\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})\cap M^{\prime}
, to define
u_{\sigma}
, let
\bar{\xi}_{\sigma}:M\to{\mathbb{R}}
be any
C^{\infty}
-map which is equal to
1
on an open
neighbourhood of
\sigma
and equal to zero on an open
neighbourhood of all other fixed points. Let
\xi_{\sigma}:M\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a
C^{\infty}
-map given by

\xi_{\sigma}:=\bar{\xi}_{\sigma}-\sum_{i=1}^{g}D_{i}(\bar{\xi}_{\sigma})f_{i}.

Then, since each
f_{i}
given by Lemma
7.8
vanishes on a neighbourhood of
Fix(\varphi_{\mathbb{R}})
(see Lemma
7.8
),
\xi_{\sigma}
is also equal to
1
on an open
neighbourhood of
\sigma
and equal to zero on an open
neighbourhood of all other fixed points. Moreover,
by linearity of the operator
\mathfrak{h}
, the definition (
7.17
) of
D_{i}
and (
7.16
),

(7.18)

\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{\xi_{\sigma}})=\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{\bar{\xi}_{\sigma}})-\sum_{i=1}^{g}D_{i}(\bar{\xi}_{\sigma})\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{f_{i}})=\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{\bar{\xi}_{\sigma}})-\sum_{i=1}^{g}d_{i}(\varphi_{\bar{\xi}_{\sigma}})h_{i}=0.

Finally, the cocycle
u_{\sigma}:{\mathbb{R}}\times M\to{\mathbb{R}}
is defined by

u_{\sigma}(T,x):=\int_{0}^{T}\xi_{\sigma}(\psi_{s}(x))\,ds.

We will show in §
7.2.3
that each
u_{i}
, in view of (
7.14
), displays the desired deviation behaviour and in §
7.2.4
that they are indeed the desired asymptotic cocycles. We first estimate the error term though.

7.2.2.
Subpolynomial deviation case

The following Proposition provides subpolynomial estimates for the growth of
corrected
ergodic integrals (in light of Corollary
6.2
) and will be used in §
7.2.4
to control the
error term
in the asymptotic expansion.

Proposition 7.9

(Subpolynomial deviation)
.

Suppose that the IET
T:I\to I
satisfies the
.
Assume that
g,\varphi_{f}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
and

\frac{1}{|I^{(k)}|}\|S(k)\varphi_{f}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}=O(e^{\tau k})\text{ for every }\tau>0.

Then
for a.e.
(x,r)\in I^{g}
we have

(7.19)

\limsup_{s\to+\infty}\frac{\log|\int_{0}^{s}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,r))dt|}{\log s}\leq 0.

Moreover, for every
p\geq 1
we have

(7.20)

\limsup_{s\to+\infty}\frac{\log\|\int_{0}^{s}f\circ T^{g}_{t}dt\|_{L^{p}(I^{g})}}{\log s}\leq 0.

Proof.

As we have already seen in Remark
7.6
, there exist two positive constants

C,b>0
such that for every
s\geq\underline{g}
we have

g(x)\leq s\text{ for all }x\in I_{s}:=\bigcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}[l_{\alpha}+Ce^{-bs},r_{\alpha}-Ce^{-bs}].

Then

\xi(s)=Leb(I\setminus I_{s})=dCe^{-bs}\quad\text{and}\quad\Xi(s)=(dC/b)e^{-bs}.

Take any
a>2
and set
\eta(s)=\frac{a}{b}\log s
. By the description of
C,b>0
, we have
[0,Ce^{-b\eta(s)}]\subset I\setminus I_{\eta(s)}
.
Hence, if
|I^{(k)}|\leq Ce^{-b\eta(s)}=C/s^{a}
then
I^{(k)}(\eta(s))=\emptyset
.
By condition (
UDC3
) and (
3.15
), it follows that

I^{(k)}(\eta(s))\neq\emptyset\Rightarrow|I^{(k)}|>C/s^{a}\Rightarrow\|Q(k)\|<\kappa s^{a}/C\Rightarrow k\leq\frac{a}{\lambda_{1}}\log(C^{\prime}s).

Moreover, if
x\in I^{(k)}(\eta(s))\cap I^{(k)}_{\alpha}
then

x\in[l^{(k)}_{\alpha}+Ce^{-b\eta(s)},r^{(k)}_{\alpha}-Ce^{-b\eta(s)}]=[l^{(k)}_{\alpha}+C/s^{a},r^{(k)}_{\alpha}-C/s^{a}].

In view of (
4.12
), (
5.15
) and (
UDC3
), it follows that for every
x\in I^{(k)}(\eta(s))
,

\displaystyle|(S(k)\varphi)(x)|

\displaystyle\leq 2\kappa\frac{\|S(k)\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}}{|I^{(k)}|}+\mathcal{LV}(S(k)\varphi)(1+\log(|I^{(k)}|s^{a}/C))

\displaystyle=O(e^{\tau k})+O(\log s\log\|Q(k)\|)=O(e^{\tau k})+O(k\log s).

Therefore, by (
7.13
), for every
(x,r)\in A^{s}_{\eta(s)}
we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{s}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,r))dt\Big|

\displaystyle\leq O(\log^{2}s)+O\Big(\sum_{0\leq k\leq\frac{a}{\lambda_{1}}\log(C^{\prime}s)}\|Z(k+1)\|e^{\tau k}\Big)+O\Big(\log s\sum_{0\leq k\leq\frac{a}{\lambda_{1}}\log(C^{\prime}s)}\|Z(k+1)\|k\Big)

\displaystyle\leq O(\log^{2}s)+O(s^{2a\tau/\lambda_{1}})+O(s^{a\tau/\lambda_{1}}\log^{2}s)=O(s^{2a\tau/\lambda_{1}}).

Moreover, by (
7.11
), we have
Leb(I^{g}\setminus A_{\eta(s)}^{s})=O(1/s^{a-1})

with

a-1>1
. Therefore, for every
\tau>0
and
a>2
there exists
C_{\tau,a}>0
such that
for every
s>0
we have

(7.21)

Leb\Big\{(x,r)\in I^{g}:\Big|\int_{0}^{s}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,r))dt\Big|>C_{\tau,a}s^{2\tau a/\lambda_{1}}\Big\}\leq Leb(I^{g}\setminus A_{\eta(s)}^{s})<\frac{C_{\tau,a}}{s^{a-1}}.

It follows that for a.e.
(x,r)\in I^{g}
we have

\limsup_{s\to+\infty}\frac{\log|\int_{0}^{s}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,r))dt|}{\log s}\leq 2\tau a/\lambda_{1}.

This gives (
7.19
).

Finally, the inequality (
7.20
) follows also directly from (
7.21
). Indeed, if
a\geq{p+1}
, then

\displaystyle\Big\|\int_{0}^{s}f\circ T^{g}_{t}dt\Big\|^{p}_{L^{p}(I^{g})}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{A_{\eta(s)}^{s}}\Big|\int_{0}^{s}f\circ T^{g}_{t}(x,r)dt\Big|^{p}\,dx\,dr+Leb(I^{g}\setminus A_{\eta(s)}^{s})s^{p}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}^{p}

\displaystyle=O(s^{2pa\tau/\lambda_{1}})+O(s^{p+1-a})=O(s^{2pa\tau/\lambda_{1}}).

∎

Corollary 7.10
.

Suppose that
T
is an IET satisfying the
and
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
. If
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi)=0
then
\int_{I}\varphi(x)\,dx=0
.

Proof.

Let us consider any roof function
g:I\to{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
such that
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
and
|\varphi(x)|\leq g(x)
for
x\in I
. Let
f:I^{g}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be given by

f(x,r)=\varphi(x)/g(x)
for
(x,r)\in I^{g}
. Then
f
is bounded and
\varphi_{f}=\varphi
.
In view of Theorem
7.9
and the ergodicity of
T
, for every
0<\tau<1
,
for a.e.
x\in I
and a.e.
r\in[0,\underline{g}]
we have

g^{(n)}(x)=O(n)\ \text{ and }\ \int_{0}^{g^{(n)}(x)}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,r))\,dt=O((g^{(n)}(x))^{\tau}).

As

\displaystyle\Big|\varphi^{(n)}(x)-\int_{0}^{g^{(n)}(x)}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,r))\,dt\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{0}^{g^{(n)}(x)}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,0))\,dt-\int_{0}^{g^{(n)}(x)}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,r))\,dt\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\int_{0}^{r}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,0))\,dt\Big|+\Big|\int_{0}^{r}f(T^{g}_{t}(T^{n}x,0))\,dt\Big|\leq 2\underline{g}\|f\|_{\sup},

it follows that
\varphi^{(n)}(x)=O(n^{\tau})
. On the other hand, for a.e.
x\in I
we have
\varphi^{(n)}(x)/n\to\int_{I}\varphi(x)\,dx
.
This gives
\int_{I}\varphi(x)\,dx=0
.
∎

7.2.3.
Pure power deviation case

We consider first a function
f
such that
\varphi_{f}=h
, where
h
has exponential growth rate
\lambda
.

Proposition 7.11

(Pure deviation)
.

Suppose that the IET
T:I\to I
satisfies the
.
Assume that the roof function
g\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
and
f:I^{g}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is a bounded function such that there exists
K>0
for which
f(x,r)=0
for
r\geq K
and
\varphi_{f}\in L^{\infty}(I)
.
Suppose that for some
\lambda\geq 0
we have

\limsup_{k\to+\infty}\frac{\log\|S(k)(\varphi_{f})\|_{L^{\infty}(I^{(k)})}}{k}\leq\lambda.

Then

(7.22)

\limsup_{s\to+\infty}\frac{\log\|\int_{0}^{s}f\circ T^{g}_{t}\,dt\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\log s}\leq\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{1}}.

If additionally
\varphi_{f}=h=(h_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\in H(\pi)
,
\lambda>0
and

\lim_{k\to+\infty}\frac{\log\|Q(k)h\|}{k}=\lambda,

then

(7.23)

\limsup_{s\to+\infty}\frac{\log\|\int_{0}^{s}f\circ T^{g}_{t}\,dt\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\log s}=\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{1}}.

Proof.

Let us consider the trimmed roof function
g_{K}:I\to[0,K]
,
g_{K}(x)=\min\{g(x),K\}
.
Taking
\eta=K
and
I_{\eta(s)}=I_{K}=I
we have
A^{s}_{\eta(s)}=I^{g_{K}}
.
Note that, by assumption, the map
\varphi_{f}
does not change after passing to the trimmed roof function.
In view of (
7.13
),
for every regular point
(x,r)\in I^{g_{K}}
we have

\Big|\int_{0}^{s}f(T^{g_{K}}_{t}(x,r))dt\Big|\leq 2\sum_{k=0}^{m(n_{K}(x,r,s))}\|Z(k+1)\|\|S(k)\varphi_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(I^{(k)})}+2\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}K^{2},

where
n_{K}(x,r,s)
is defined by (
7.1
) for the roof
g_{K}
. Then

0\leq n_{K}(x,r,s)\leq n(x,r,s)\leq s/\underline{g}+1.

By assumption, for every
\tau>0
we have

\|S(k)\varphi_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(I^{(k)})}=O(e^{(\lambda+\tau)k}).

Moreover, by (
7.4
),

e^{\lambda_{1}m(n_{K}(x,r,s))}=O(n_{K}(x,r,s)^{1+\tau})=O(s^{1+\tau}).

Therefore, by (
3.17
), it follows that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{s}f(T^{g_{K}}_{t}(x,r))dt\Big|

\displaystyle\leq O\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{m(n_{K}(x,r,s))}\|Z(k+1)\|\|S(k)\varphi_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(I^{(k)})}+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}K^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=O\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{m(n_{K}(x,r,s))}e^{(\lambda+2\tau)k}+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}K^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=O\big(e^{(\lambda+2\tau)m(n_{K}(x,r,s))}+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}K^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=O(s^{(\lambda+2\tau)(1+\tau)}).

By assumption and the definition of
g_{K}
, for every regular
(x,r)\in I^{g}
and
s>0
there exists
0\leq s^{\prime}=s^{\prime}(x,r,s)\leq s
such that

\Big|\int_{0}^{s}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,r))dt\Big|=\Big|\int_{0}^{s^{\prime}}f(T^{g_{K}}_{t}(x,r))dt\Big|=O\big({(s^{\prime})}^{(\lambda+2\tau)(1+\tau)}\big)=O(s^{(\lambda+2\tau)(1+\tau)}).

This gives (
7.22
) and proves one inequality (namely the upper bound) in (
7.23
).

To prove the inverse inequality and therefore (
7.23
), note that for every
x\in I^{(k)}_{\alpha}
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{S(k)g(x)}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,0))\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{g^{(Q_{\alpha}(k))}(x)}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,0))\,dt=\varphi_{f}^{(Q_{\alpha}(k))}(x)=S(k)\varphi_{f}(x)=(Q(k)h)_{\alpha}.

Moreover, by assumption, for every
\tau>0
there exists
c>0
such that for every
k\geq 0
we have

\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}|(Q(k)h)_{\alpha}|=\|Q(k)h\|\geq ce^{\lambda(1-\tau)k}.

As
g
is positive, by (
B1
), (
3.5
) and (
UDC3
), we have

\displaystyle m(S(k)g,I^{(k)}_{\alpha})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|I^{(k)}|}{|I^{(k)}_{\alpha}|}m(S(k)g,I^{(k)})\leq\frac{\kappa m(g,I)}{|I^{(k)}|}\leq\kappa m(g,I)\|Q(k)\|\leq\kappa m(g,I)Ce^{\lambda_{1}(1+\tau)k}.

For every
k\geq 0
choose
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}
such that
|(Q(k)h)_{\alpha}|=\tfrac{1}{d}\|Q(k)h\|
and then we take any

x^{(k)}\in I^{(k)}_{\alpha}
such that
s_{k}:=S(k)g(x^{(k)})\leq\kappa m(g,I)Ce^{\lambda_{1}(1+\tau)k}
. Then

\displaystyle\Big\|\int_{0}^{s_{k}}f\circ T^{g}_{t}\,dt\Big\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\geq\Big|\int_{0}^{S(k)g(x^{(k)})}f(T^{g}_{t}(x^{(k)},0))\,dt\Big|=|(Q(k)h)_{\alpha}|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\|Q(k)h\|\geq\frac{c}{d}e^{\lambda(1-\tau)k}\geq\frac{c}{d(\kappa m(g,I)C)^{\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{1}}\frac{1-\tau}{1+\tau}}}(s_{k})^{\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{1}}\frac{1-\tau}{1+\tau}k}.

It follows that for every
\tau>0
we have

\limsup_{s\to+\infty}\frac{\log\|\int_{0}^{s}f\circ T^{g}_{t}\,dt\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\log s}\geq\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{1}}\frac{1-\tau}{1+\tau},

which gives (
7.23
).
∎

To have uniform control over the asymptotics of the error growth, we also need the following Corollary.

Corollary 7.12
.

Let
T:I\to I
is an IET satisfying the
and
g\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
be a roof function. Suppose that
f:I^{g}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is a bounded function such that
\varphi_{f},\varphi_{|f|}\in L^{\infty}(I)
.
Then for every
\lambda\geq 0
,

(7.24)

\limsup_{k\to+\infty}\frac{\log\|S(k)(\varphi_{f})\|_{L^{\infty}(I^{(k)})}}{k}\leq\lambda\ \Longrightarrow\ \limsup_{s\to+\infty}\frac{\log\|\int_{0}^{s}f\circ T^{g}_{t}\,dt\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\log s}\leq\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{1}}.

Proof.

For any
K>0
let us consider the bounded map
f_{K}:I^{g}\to{\mathbb{R}}
given by

f_{K}(x,r)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccl}f(x,r)&\text{if}&g(x)\leq K\\
\varphi_{f}(x)/K&\text{if}&g(x)>K\text{ and }r\leq g(x)\\
0&\text{if}&g(x)>K\text{ and }r>g(x).\end{array}\right.

Then
f_{K}
satisfies the assumptions of the first part of Proposition
7.11
and
\varphi_{f_{K}}=\varphi_{f}
. Hence

(7.25)

\limsup_{s\to+\infty}\frac{\log\|\int_{0}^{s}f_{K}\circ T^{g}_{t}\,dt\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\log s}\leq\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{1}}.

Note that for every
x\in I
in the interior of exchanged intervals and any pair
0\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq g(x)
we have

\displaystyle|\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}f(x,r)\,dr|\leq\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}|f(x,r)|\,dr\leq\int_{0}^{g(x)}|f(x,r)|\,dr=\varphi_{|f|}(x)\leq\|\varphi_{|f|}\|_{\sup},

\displaystyle|\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}f_{K}(x,r)\,dr|\leq\varphi_{|f_{K}|}(x)|\leq\varphi_{|f|}(x)\leq\|\varphi_{|f|}\|_{\sup}.

As

\int_{0}^{g(x)}f_{K}(x,r)\,dr=\varphi_{f_{K}}(x)=\varphi_{f}(x)=\int_{0}^{g(x)}f(x,r)\,dr,

it follows that for every regular point
(x,r)\in I^{g}
and any
s>0
we have

\Big|\int_{0}^{s}f(T^{g}_{t}(x,r))\,dt-\int_{0}^{s}f_{K}(T^{g}_{t}(x,r))\,dt\Big|\leq 4\|\varphi_{|f|}\|_{\sup}.

Together with (
7.25
) this yields (
7.24
).
∎

7.2.4.
Power deviation spectrum

Combining the results in the two previous subsections, we can now prove the full deviation spectrum result stated in Theorem
1.4
as well as the existence of the asymptotic expansion in Theorem
1.3
.

Proof of Theorem
1.4
and of the first part of Theorem
1.3
.

Let
D_{i},1\leq i\leq g
,
u_{i},1\leq i\leq g
and
u_{\sigma}
,
\sigma\in\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})\cap M^{\prime}
, be respectively the distributions and the cocycles defined in §
7.2.1
. One can see that, for each
1\leq i\leq g
,
u_{i}
displays the desired power behaviour (
1.6
), by the pure deviation Theorem
7.11
proved in §
7.2.3
, which can be applied to
f=f_{i}
since by construction
\varphi_{f_{i}}=h_{i}
and
h_{i}
has exponential growth rate
\lambda_{i}
, see (
7.14
).

The error term function.

Let us consider
f_{e}\in C^{2+\epsilon}(M)
given by

f_{e}:=f-\sum_{i=1}^{g}D_{i}(f)f_{i}.

By the definition of
f_{i}
,
i=1,\ldots,g
,

(7.26)

f_{e}(\sigma)=f(\sigma)\text{ for every }\sigma\in\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})\cap M^{\prime}.

Then we set

err(f,T,x):=\int_{0}^{T}f_{e}(\psi_{s}(x))\,ds.

Let
\varphi_{f_{e}}
be the cocycle associated to
f_{e}
(refer to §
2.3.3
).
We can then check that
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{f_{e}})=0
, since

(7.27)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{f_{e}})=\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{f})-\sum_{i=1}^{g}D_{i}(f)\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{f_{i}})=\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{f})-\sum_{i=1}^{g}D_{i}(f)\mathfrak{h}(h_{i})=\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{f})-\sum_{i=1}^{g}d_{i}(\varphi_{f})h_{i}=0.\end{split}

We now show that for every non-zero
\xi\in C^{2+\epsilon}(M)
such that
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{\xi})=0

we have

(7.28)

\displaystyle\limsup_{T\to+\infty}\frac{\log|\int_{0}^{T}\xi(\psi_{t}(x))\,dt|}{\log T}=0\text{ for a.e. }x\in M^{\prime},\quad\limsup_{T\to+\infty}\frac{\log\|\int_{0}^{T}\xi\circ\psi_{t}\,dt\|_{L^{p}(M^{\prime})}}{\log T}=0.

As
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{\xi})=0
, in view of Corollary
6.2
,
we can apply the subpolynomial deviation Theorem
7.9
to
f=\xi
and prove both inequalities
\leq
in (
7.28
).

Almost everywhere error estimates.
Suppose now that the left equality in (
7.28
) does not hold. Then there exists a subset
B\subset M^{\prime}
with positive area such that

\lim_{T\to+\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\xi(\psi_{t}(x))\,dt=0\text{ for all }x\in B.

By the ergodicity of the flow, for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in M^{\prime}
, the limit

\zeta(x)=\lim_{T\to+\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\xi(\psi_{t}(x))\,dt\text{ exists.}

Then
\zeta:M^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is a measurable map such that
\zeta(x)=0
for any
x\in B
and

(7.29)

\zeta(x)-\zeta(\psi_{s}x)=\int_{0}^{s}\xi(\psi_{t}(x))\,dt\text{ for every }s>0\text{ and a.e. }x\in M^{\prime}.

Note that
\zeta\equiv 0
. Indeed, by definition and (
7.29
), for a.e.
x\in M^{\prime}
we have
\lim_{s\to+\infty}\zeta(\psi_{s}x)=0
.
Since
\psi_{\mathbb{R}}
is ergodic, this gives
\zeta\equiv 0
. Therefore,

\frac{1}{s}\int_{0}^{s}\xi(\psi_{t}(x))\,dt=\frac{1}{s}(\zeta(x)-\zeta(\psi_{s}x))=0\text{ for every }s>0\text{ and a.e. }x\in M^{\prime}.

As
\xi
is continuous, it follows that for a.e.
x\in M^{\prime}
we have

\xi(x)=\lim_{s\to 0}\frac{1}{s}\int_{0}^{s}\xi(\psi_{t}(x))\,dt=0,

contrary to the assumption
\xi
is non-zero.

Error estimates in
L^{p}
norm.
Suppose now
that the right equality in (
7.28
) does not hold. Then

\lim_{T\to+\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\xi\circ\psi_{t}\,dt=0\text{ in }L^{p}.

Hence, for every
s>0
we have

\int_{0}^{s}\xi\circ\psi_{t}\,dt=\lim_{T\to+\infty}\int_{0}^{T+s}\xi\circ\psi_{t}\,dt-\lim_{T\to+\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\xi\circ\psi_{t}\circ\psi_{s}\,dt=0

in
L^{p}
. It follows that
\frac{1}{s}\int_{0}^{s}\xi(\psi_{t}(x))\,dt=0
for every
s>0
and a.e.
x\in M^{\prime}
. The final contradiction argument is the same as above. This completes the proof of (
7.28
).

In view of (
7.18
) and (
7.27
),
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{\xi_{\sigma}})=0
and
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{f_{e}})=0
, so we can apply
(
7.28
) to
\xi=\xi_{\sigma}
and
\xi=f_{e}
. This yields (
1.7
) in in Theorem
1.3
as well as (
1.12
) and (
1.13
) in Theorem
1.4
.

Uniform estimates of
err_{b}
.

Let us consider
f_{eb}\in C^{2+\epsilon}(M)
given by

(7.30)

f_{eb}=f_{e}-\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})\cap M^{\prime}}f(\sigma)\xi_{\sigma}.

Then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}f_{eb}(\psi_{t}x)\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}f_{e}(\psi_{t}x)\,dt-\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})\cap M^{\prime}}f(\sigma)\int_{0}^{T}\xi_{\sigma}(\psi_{t}x)\,dt

\displaystyle=err(f,T,x)-\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})\cap M^{\prime}}f(\sigma)u_{\sigma}(T,x)=err_{b}(f,T,x).

Since
f_{e}(\sigma)=f(\sigma)
and
\xi_{\sigma}(\sigma^{\prime})=\delta_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}
, for every
\sigma\in\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})\cap M^{\prime}
we have

(7.31)

f_{eb}(\sigma)=f_{e}(\sigma)-\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})\cap M^{\prime}}f(\sigma^{\prime})\xi_{\sigma^{\prime}}(\sigma)=0.

In view of Proposition
4.1
,
\varphi_{f_{be}}\in{\operatorname{AC}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
. As
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{\xi_{\sigma}})=0
and
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{f_{e}})=0
, we also have

(7.32)

\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{f_{eb}})=\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{f_{e}})-\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})\cap M^{\prime}}f(\sigma)\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{\xi_{\sigma}})=0.

In view of (
4.13
), the property (
B1
) of the
, (
5.3
) and Corollary
6.2
, it follows that for every
\tau>0
we have

\displaystyle\|S(k)\varphi_{f_{eb}}\|_{\sup}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|I^{(k)}|}{\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}|I_{\alpha}^{(k)}|}\frac{\|S(k)\varphi_{f_{eb}}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}}{|I^{(k)}|}+\operatorname{Var}(S(k)\varphi_{f_{eb}})

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\frac{\|S(k)\varphi_{f_{eb}}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}}{|I^{(k)}|}+\operatorname{Var}(\varphi_{f_{eb}})=O(e^{\tau k}).

By (
7.31
) and Proposition
4.1
(see in particular property
(i)
), the map
\varphi_{|f|}:I\to{\mathbb{R}}
is bounded. In view of Corollary
7.12
, this gives (
1.11
).

This completes the proof of Theorem
1.4
as well as the proof of the first part of Theorem
1.3
.
∎

The second part of the statement of Theorem
1.3
, namely the equidistribution statement for the error term (which is a consequence of ergodicity) and the uniform estimates on
err_{b}
will be proved at the end, in §
8.2.4
.

8.
Ergodicity of extensions

The goal of this section is to prove Main Theorem
1.2
and complete the proof of Main Theorem
1.3
. In view of the reduction explained in §
2.3.3
and the equivalence between ergodicity
of the extension
\Phi^{f}_{\mathbb{R}}
on
M\times{\mathbb{R}}
and of the skew product
T_{\varphi_{f}}
on
I\times{\mathbb{R}}
obtained via a Poincaré first return, we treat first the case of skew products of this form. The main result on ergodicity of skew products is Theorem
8.1
stated in §
8.1
below.
In §
8.1.1
we state the ergodicity criterium that we will use to prove it (see Proposition
8.2
). Theorem
8.1
is then proved in §
8.1.2
. Finally, in §
8.2
we prove Main Theorem
1.2
, by combining the ergodicity result for skew products with a discussion on reducibility.

8.1.
Ergodicity of skew products over IETs with logarithmic singularities

We state in this section the ergodicity result for skew-products over IETs with cocycles with logarithmic singularities. We also show that the ergodicity result for locally Hamiltonian flows (Main Theorem
1.2
) can be reduced to it.

Theorem 8.1

(Ergodicity of skew-products with log-singularities over IETs)
.

Suppose that
T:I\to I
satisfies the
. Let
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
be a cocycle with logarithmic singularities of geometric type so that

\mathcal{L}(\varphi)>0,\qquad\mathcal{AS}(\varphi)=0,\qquad g^{\prime}_{\varphi}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha}).

Assume furthermore that

\varphi
is
corrected
, namely
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi)=0
.
Then the skew product
T_{\varphi}
on
I\times{\mathbb{R}}
is ergodic.

The proof of the Theorem will take most of the section, from §
8.1
to the end.
We first state the ergodicity criterium which will be exploited (see §
8.1.1
) and proceed with the proof, which will take §
8.1.2
.

8.1.1.
An ergodicity criterium

We now formulate a quite classical criterium (Proposition
8.2
) for ergodicity of a special flow. It shows that one can deduce the existence of
essential values
(a classical tool to prove ergodicity, see e.g.
[
1
,
59
]
) to the presence of
rigidity sets
for the base transformation on which Birkhoff sums (up to the time which gives rigidity) are
tight
. The criterium was in particular used (and proved) in
[
20
]
. For simplicity in this section we constantly assume that
|I|=1
.

We first give the definition of
rigidity sequence
for IETs (which are the base transformations in the special flow).

Definition 9

(Rigidity sequences for an IET)
.

Let
T:I\to I
be an IET. Let
(\Xi_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be a sequence of towers of intervals of the form
\Xi_{n}=\{T^{i}J_{n}:0\leq i<p_{n}\}
.
We say that
(\Xi_{n})_{n\geq 1}
is a
rigid sequence of towers
if there exists a strictly increasing sequence
(q_{n})_{n\geq 1}
,
called the
rigidity sequence
, and
\delta>0
such that

\displaystyle Leb(\Xi_{n})\geq\delta\text{ and }\sup_{x\in\Xi_{n}}|T^{q_{n}}x-x|\to 0.

The following Proposition is the ergodicity criterium that we will exploit. It was proved in
[
20
]
(using Proposition 2.3 and the end of the proof of Proposition 5.2 in
[
20
]
).

Proposition 8.2

(Ergodicity criterium, see
[
20
]
)
.

Assume that
T:I\to I
is an ergodic IET and
\varphi:I\to{\mathbb{R}}
a measurable map.
Suppose that
(\Xi_{n})_{n\geq 1}
is a rigid sequence of tower and
(q_{n})_{n\geq 1}
its rigidity sequence.
If for all
|s|\geq s_{0}
we have

(8.1)

\int_{\Xi_{n}}|\varphi^{(q_{n})}(x)|\,dx=O(1)\text{ and }\int_{\Xi_{n}}e^{2\pi s\varphi^{(q_{n})}(x)}\,dx=\frac{2}{3}Leb(\Xi_{n})+O(|s|^{-1}),

then the skew product
T_{\varphi}
on
I\times{\mathbb{R}}
is ergodic.

Remark 8.3
.

Suppose that
T
satisfies the
. For every
k\geq 1
let
J^{(k)}\subset I^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}}
be a sequence of intervals such that
\liminf|J^{(k)}|/|I^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}}|>0
. Then
\Xi_{k}=\{T^{i}J^{(k)}:0\leq i<p_{k}\}
establishes a rigid sequence of towers with the rigidity sequence given by
q_{k}:=Q_{\alpha_{k}}(k)
.
It follows directly from (
B1
) and (
B2
). Since
T^{q_{k}}J^{(k)}\subset I^{(k)}
, for every
0\leq l\leq q_{k}
we have that
T^{l}\Xi_{k}=\{T^{l+i}J^{(k)}:0\leq i<p_{k}\}

is also a tower of intervals.

Specializing the ergodicity criterion to our setting, we have the following Proposition, that shows that to prove ergodicity (and Theorem
8.1
) it is sufficient to verify the assumptions in the statement:

Proposition 8.4
.

Suppose that
T
satisfies the
and let
(\Xi_{k})_{k\geq 1}
and
(q_{k})_{k\geq 1}
be a sequence of rigid towers and its rigidity sequence as in Remark
8.3
.
Let
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
be a map such that
g^{\prime}_{\varphi}\in{\operatorname{BV}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
.
We additionally assume that there exists
c>0
such that

(i)

the sequence
\frac{1}{|I^{(k)}|}\|S(k)\varphi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}
is bounded;

(ii)

\operatorname{dist}\big(\bigcup_{i=0}^{q_{k}}T^{i}\Xi_{k},End(T)\big)\geq c/q_{k}
;

(iii)

for every
0\leq j<p_{k}
there exists an interval
J^{(k)}_{j}\subset T^{j}J^{(k)}
such that

|J^{(k)}_{j}|\geq|J^{(k)}|/3
and

|(\varphi^{\prime})^{(q_{k})}(x)|\geq cq_{k}
for all
x\in J^{(k)}_{j}
.

Then the skew product
T_{\varphi}
on
I\times{\mathbb{R}}
is ergodic.

The proof is a variation on arguments from
[
20
]
. We present it for completeness in the Appendix
A.1
.

Remark 8.5
.

One can see from the proof presented in Appendix
A.1
that the same conclusion about the ergodicity of the skew product can be deduced under the assumption that
conditions
(i)
,
(ii)
and
(iii)
hold along any subsequence.

8.1.2.
Proof of ergodicity of skew products

We will now prove Theorem
8.1
by showing that the assumptions of the criterion for ergodicity of skew products with logarithmic singularities over IETs (namely Proposition
8.4
) hold.

For every
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
such that
\mathcal{AS}(\varphi)=0
and
\mathcal{L}(\varphi)>0
(using the definitions introduced in §
4
) we want to construct
a sequence of rigid towers as in Remark
8.3
for which the condition
(ii)
and
(iii)
in Proposition
8.4
hold.
In view of (
B2
) and
[
20
, Lemma 5.1]
, we have the following result:

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
be such that
\mathcal{AS}(\varphi)=0
and
\mathcal{L}(\varphi)>0
.
There exists a sequence
(\alpha_{k})_{k\geq 1}
in
\mathcal{A}
and a sequence on natural number
(j_{k})_{k\geq 1}
such that

p_{k}\leq j_{k}<Q_{\alpha_{k}}(k)
and at least one of the following cases hold:

(L):

C_{\alpha_{0}}^{+}\neq 0
and
T^{j_{k}}l^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}}=l_{\alpha_{0}}
or

(R):

C_{\alpha_{0}}^{-}\neq 0
and
\widehat{T}^{j_{k}}r^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}}=r_{\alpha_{0}}
.

Moreover, the closures of the intervals
T^{j}I^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}}
for
Q_{\alpha_{k}}(k)\leq j<Q_{\alpha_{k}}(k)+p_{k}

do not intersect
End(T)
.

Definition 10
.

For any
0\leq\bar{c}<1/2
we define the base
J^{(k)}\subset I^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}}=[a,b)
of the tower
\Xi_{k}
as follows:

\displaystyle J^{(k)}=\left(a+\frac{\bar{c}}{2}\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}},a+\bar{c}\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}}\right)

\displaystyle\text{ in case }(L);

\displaystyle J^{(k)}=\left(a-\bar{c}\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}},b-\frac{\bar{c}}{2}\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}}\right)

\displaystyle\text{ in case }(R).

Lemma 8.7
.

Let
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
be such that
\mathcal{AS}(\varphi)=0
and
\mathcal{L}(\varphi)>0
and
g^{\prime}_{\varphi},g^{\prime\prime}_{\varphi}\in{\operatorname{AC}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
. Let
(\Xi_{k})_{k\geq 1}
be a sequence of rigid towers defined in Definition
10
with

(8.2)

\bar{c}=\sqrt{\frac{|C^{\pm}_{\alpha_{0}}|}{2(6\mathcal{L}(\varphi)+\operatorname{Var}g^{\prime}_{\varphi})}}.

Then

|(\varphi^{\prime\prime})^{(q_{k})}(x)|\geq\frac{\underline{c}}{(\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}})^{2}}\text{ for all }x\in\Xi_{k},

where
\underline{c}=6\mathcal{L}(\varphi)+\operatorname{Var}g^{\prime}_{\varphi}
.

Proof.

We present the proof only in the case
(L)
. The other case is similar.

Suppose that
x\in T^{l}J^{(k)}
for some
0\leq l<p_{k}
. By assumption,

\frac{|C_{\alpha_{0}}^{+}|}{\{T^{j_{k}-l}x-l_{\alpha_{0}}\}^{2}}\geq\frac{|C_{\alpha_{0}}^{+}|}{\bar{c}^{2}(\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}})^{2}},

the elements of the orbit
T^{j}x
for
0\leq j<p_{k}
are distant from each other at least
\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}}
and for
j\neq j_{k}-l
we have
\{T^{j}x-l_{\alpha_{0}}\}\geq\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}}
. It follows that

\sum_{0\leq j<p_{k},\ j\neq j_{k}-l}\frac{|C_{\alpha_{0}}^{+}|}{\{T^{j}x-l_{\alpha_{0}}\}^{2}}\leq\frac{|C_{\alpha_{0}}^{+}|}{(\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}})^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{k}}\frac{1}{j^{2}}\leq\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\frac{|C_{\alpha_{0}}^{+}|}{(\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}})^{2}}\leq 2\frac{|C_{\alpha_{0}}^{+}|}{(\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}})^{2}}.

Since for every
0\leq j<p_{k}
we have
\{T^{j}x-l_{\alpha}\}\geq\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}}
for
\alpha\neq\alpha_{k}
and

\{r_{\alpha}-T^{j}x\}\geq\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}}/2
for all
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}
, the same arguments show that for all
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}

we have

\sum_{0\leq j<p_{k}}\frac{|C_{\alpha}^{-}|}{\{r_{\alpha}-T^{j}x\}^{2}}\leq 6\frac{|C_{\alpha}^{-}|}{(\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}})^{2}}\text{ and }\sum_{0\leq j<p_{k}}\frac{|C_{\alpha}^{+}|}{\{T^{j}x-l_{\alpha}\}^{2}}\leq 2\frac{|C_{\alpha}^{+}|}{(\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}})^{2}}\text{ if }\alpha\neq\alpha_{k}.

Moreover, for every
x\in I
we have

|(g^{\prime\prime}_{\varphi})^{(q_{k})}(x)|\leq q_{k}\|g^{\prime\prime}_{\varphi}\|_{\sup}\leq\frac{\operatorname{Var}g^{\prime}_{\varphi}}{(\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}})^{2}}.

As

\varphi^{\prime\prime}(x)=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{C_{\alpha}^{+}}{\{x-l_{\alpha}\}^{2}}+\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{C_{\alpha}^{-}}{\{r_{\alpha}-x\}^{2}}+g^{\prime\prime}_{\varphi}(x),

it follows that for every
x\in\Xi_{k}
we have

\displaystyle|(\varphi^{\prime\prime})^{(q_{k})}(x)|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{|C_{\alpha_{0}}^{+}|}{\bar{c}^{2}(\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}})^{2}}-\frac{6\mathcal{L}(\varphi)}{(\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}})^{2}}-\frac{\operatorname{Var}g^{\prime}_{\varphi}}{(\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}})^{2}}=\Big(\frac{|C_{\alpha_{0}}^{+}|}{\bar{c}^{2}}-6\mathcal{L}(\varphi)-\operatorname{Var}g^{\prime}_{\varphi}\Big)\frac{1}{(\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}})^{2}}=\frac{\underline{c}}{(\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}})^{2}}.

∎

The following elementary lemma will help us to choose the subintervals
J^{(k)}_{l}\subset T^{l}J^{(k)}
satisfying
condition
(iii)
in Proposition
8.4
.

Lemma 8.8
.

Let
f:I\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a
C^{1}
map defined on a closed interval
I
and such that
|f^{\prime}(x)|\geq c>0
for all
x\in I
.
Then there exists a closed subinterval
J\subset I
such that
|J|\geq|I|/3
and
|f(x)|\geq c|I|/6
for all
x\in I
.

Proof of Theorem
8.1
.

Recall that
\varphi\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
is a cocycle such that

\mathcal{L}(\varphi)>0,\quad\mathcal{AS}(\varphi)=0,\quad\mathfrak{h}(\varphi)=0\quad\text{and}\quad g^{\prime}_{\varphi}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha}).

By definition,
\varphi=\varphi_{0}+g_{\varphi}
, where

{\varphi}_{0}(x)=-\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}C_{\alpha}^{+}{\log}\{x-l_{\alpha}\}-\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}C_{\alpha}^{-}{\log}\{r_{\alpha}-x\}

and
g_{\varphi}\in{\operatorname{AC}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
with
g^{\prime}_{\varphi}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
.
By Proposition
8.9
,
g_{\varphi}
is cohomologous to a piecewise linear map

\psi\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
with
\mathfrak{h}(\psi)=\mathfrak{h}(g_{\varphi})
.
It follows that
\varphi
is cohomologous to
\bar{\varphi}:=\varphi_{0}+\psi
. Then
\bar{\varphi}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
is such that
\mathcal{L}(\bar{\varphi})=\mathcal{L}({\varphi}_{0})=\mathcal{L}(\varphi)>0
,
\mathcal{AS}(\bar{\varphi})=\mathcal{AS}({\varphi}_{0})=\mathcal{AS}(\varphi)=0
,

\mathfrak{h}(\bar{\varphi})=\mathfrak{h}({\varphi}_{0})+\mathfrak{h}(\psi)=\mathfrak{h}({\varphi}_{0})+\mathfrak{h}(g_{\varphi})=\mathfrak{h}({\varphi})=0

and
g_{\bar{\varphi}}=\psi
, so
g^{\prime}_{\bar{\varphi}},g^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{\varphi}}\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
.
As
\varphi
is cohomologous to
\bar{\varphi}
, the skew products
T_{\varphi}
and
T_{\bar{\varphi}}
are isomorphic, so it is sufficient to show
the ergodicity of
T_{\bar{\varphi}}
.

Let
(\Xi_{k})_{k\geq 1}
be a sequence of rigid towers defined in Definition
10
with
\bar{c}
given by (
8.2
)
for that function
\bar{\varphi}
. In view of (
B2
) and (
3.16
), this sequence satisfies

\operatorname{dist}\Big(\bigcup_{i=0}^{q_{k}}T^{i}\Xi_{k},End(T)\Big)\geq\frac{1}{2}\bar{c}\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}}\geq\frac{\delta\bar{c}}{2\kappa q_{k}},

so condition
(ii)
in Proposition
8.4
holds. Moreover, by Lemma
8.7
,

|(\bar{\varphi}^{\prime\prime})^{(q_{n})}(x)|\geq\frac{\underline{c}}{(\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}})^{2}}\text{ for every }x\in T^{l}J^{(k)},\ 0\leq l<p_{k}.

Since
|T^{l}J^{(k)}|=|J^{(k)}|=\frac{\bar{c}}{2}\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}}
, by Lemma
8.8
, for every
0\leq l<p_{k}
there exists
an interval
J^{(k)}_{l}\subset T^{l}J^{(k)}
such that

|J^{(k)}_{l}|\geq|J^{(k)}|/3\text{ and }|(\bar{\varphi}^{\prime})^{(q_{n})}(x)|\geq\frac{\underline{c}}{(\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}})^{2}}\frac{\bar{c}}{12}\lambda^{(k)}_{\alpha_{k}}\geq\frac{\underline{c}\bar{c}}{12}q_{k}\text{ for every }x\in J^{(k)}_{l},

so condition
(iii)
in Proposition
8.4
holds.

Since
\mathcal{AS}(\bar{\varphi})=0
and
\mathfrak{h}(\bar{\varphi})=0
, by Theorem
6.1
and (
3.27
) in Proposition
3.9
,

\frac{\|S(r_{n})\bar{\varphi}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(r_{n})})}}{|I^{(r_{n})}|}\text{ is bounded,}

so condition
(i)
in Proposition
8.4
holds along a subsequence. In view of Proposition
8.4
together with Remark
8.5
,
this gives the ergodicity of
T_{\bar{\varphi}}
, and hence the ergodicity of
T_{{\varphi}}
.
∎

8.2.
Reducibility and final arguments

The main goal of this section is to prove Main Theorem
1.2
, in particular the dichotomy between ergodicity and reducibility for typical extensions with observables in a suitable subspace of smooth functions. We also deduce from Main Theorem
1.2
the second and final part of Main Theorem
1.3
. We first need to state an auxiliary result that we call
cohomological reduction
.

8.2.1.
Cohomological reduction

The following result allows to reduce the study of cocycles whose derivatives have logarithmic singularities (up to coboundaries and hence cohomological equivalence) to piecewise linear cocycles (whose derivative is piecewise-constant). An analogous result was proved also in
[
20
]
, but only in the special measure zero class of self-similar IETs considered there.

Theorem 8.9
.

Assume that
T
satisfies the
. Then every

\varphi\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
with
\varphi^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})

is cohomologous (via a bounded transfer function)
to a piecewise linear cocycle
\psi\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
with
\mathfrak{h}(\psi)=\mathfrak{h}(\varphi)
,

\partial_{\pi}(\psi)=\partial_{\pi}(\varphi)
and
\|S(k)(\varphi-\psi)\|_{\sup}
tends to
0
exponentially.

The proof of the theorem, which generalizes the proof in
[
20
]
to full measure, is included in the Appendix
A.2
.
In view of Theorem A in
[
44
]
, if
T
is a Roth-type IET and
\varphi\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
is such that
s(\varphi)=0
and

\varphi^{\prime}\in\operatorname{BV}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
, then
\varphi
is cohomologous (via a bounded transfer function) to a piecewise constant map
h
and
\|S(k)(\varphi-h)\|_{\sup}
tends to
0
exponentially.

Assume additionally that
\varphi
in Theorem
8.9
satisfies
s(\varphi)=0
. Then

s(\psi)=\sum_{\mathcal{O}\in\Sigma(\pi)}(\partial_{\pi}(\psi))_{\mathcal{O}}=\sum_{\mathcal{O}\in\Sigma(\pi)}(\partial_{\pi}(\varphi))_{\mathcal{O}}=s(\varphi)=0.

So, by Theorem A in
[
44
]
, it follows that
\psi
is cohomologous to piecewise constant map. As the
implies Roth-type (see Remark
3.7
), this gives the following important corollary, which gives a generalization of Theorem A in
[
44
]
.

Corollary 8.10
.

Assume that
T
satisfies the
. Then every

\varphi\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
with
s(\varphi)=0
and

\varphi^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})

is cohomologous (via a bounded transfer function) to a piecewise constant map

h
and
\|S(k)(\varphi-h)\|_{\sup}
tends to
0
exponentially.

The importance of this result is that in view of Proposition
4.1
it applies to solve cohomological equations
for a.e.
\psi_{\mathbb{R}}\in\mathscr{U}_{\min}
