and for functions
f\in C^{2+\epsilon}(M)
vanishing on
\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})
. Recall that
Theorem A in
[
44
]
applies only when
f
vanishes on an open neighborhood of
\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})
.

Classical
Gottschalk-Hedlund type arguments, first applied in the context of IETs in
[
44
, §3.4]
, show the following.

Lemma 8.11

(
[
44
]
)
.

Suppose that
T:I\to I
is a minimal IET and
\varphi\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
.
The following conditions are equivalent:

(i)

the sequence
\|\varphi^{(n)}\|_{\sup}
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, is bounded;

(ii)

\varphi=g-g\circ T
, where
g:I\to{\mathbb{R}}
is bounded;

(iii)

\varphi=g-g\circ T
, where
g:I\to{\mathbb{R}}
is bounded and has at most countably many discontinuities.

Proof.

The implications follow from the classical Gottschalk-Hedlund theorem, that can be applied to IETs by extending them to a homeomorphism to a Cantor space, see
[
44
, §3.4]
.
The only non-classical implication,
(iii)
\Rightarrow
(i)
is also proved in
[
44
, §3.4]
, where the authors show that the transfer map
g
exists and is the composition of a continuous map
and a monotonic map, so is bounded and has at most countably many discontinuities.
∎

8.2.2.
Reduction to coboundaries.

Now that we reduced to the study of cocycles which are piecewise absolutely continuous (i.e. to
\varphi\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
), we can prove reducibility exploiting the following result on coboundaries.

Proposition 8.12
.

Assume that
T
satisfies the
. Then every

\varphi\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
with
\partial_{\pi}(\varphi)=0
,
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi)=0
and

\varphi^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})

is a coboundary with a bounded transfer map having at most countably many discontinuities.

Proof.

By Corollary
8.10
, there exists
h\in\Gamma
and a bounded map
g:I\to{\mathbb{R}}

such that
\varphi=h+g-g\circ T
. Moreover,

\|S(k)(\varphi-h)\|_{\sup}
tends to
0
exponentially. As

\|\partial_{\pi}(\varphi-h)\|=\|\partial_{\pi^{(k)}}(S(k)(\varphi-h))\|\leq 2d\|S(k)(\varphi-h)\|_{\sup},

it follows that
\partial_{\pi}(h)=\partial_{\pi}(\varphi)=0
, so
h\in H(\pi)
. Moreover, as

\frac{\|S(k)(\varphi-h)\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}}{|I^{(k)}|}\leq\|S(k)(\varphi-h)\|_{\sup}\to 0,

by the definition of the operator
\mathfrak{h}
and Corollary
6.2
, we have
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi-h)=0
.
It follows that
\mathfrak{h}(h)=\mathfrak{h}(\varphi)=0
, so
h\in\Gamma_{s}
. Therefore,
h
is also a coboundary with a bounded transfer map. As the sum of two coboundaries,
\varphi=h+g-g\circ T

is also a coboundary with a bounded transfer map. Finally, in view of Lemma
8.11
, the transfer map has at most countably many discontinuities.
∎

8.2.3.
Proof of the dichotomy for extensions.

We have now all ingredients needed for the proof of the dichotomy in Main Theorem
1.2
.

Proof of Main Theorem
1.2
.

Let us say that a locally Hamiltonian flows
\psi_{\mathbb{R}}
satisfies the
condition if and only if
\psi_{\mathbb{R}}
has a section
I\subset M
such that the corresponding IET
T
satisfies the condition
. Then, since the
has full measure by Theorems
3.8
and
5.6
, one can show
by definition of the measure class on
\mathscr{U}_{min}
(see for example
[
63
]
)
that the set of locally Hamiltonian flows satisfying the condition

has full measure in
\mathscr{U}_{min}
(in the sense of §
2.1.2
).

In view of Propositions
2.4
and
2.5
, we equivalently need to prove the dichotomy between ergodicity and reducibility for the skew product map
T_{\varphi_{f}}
. Furthermore, we know from Proposition
4.1
that the cocycle
\varphi_{f}
is such that

\varphi_{f}\in\operatorname{LG}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
,
\partial_{\pi}(\varphi_{f})=0
,

g^{\prime}_{\varphi_{f}}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
and
\mathcal{AS}(\varphi_{f})=0
.

Definition of the subspace
K
.

Let us consider the linear operator
\mathfrak{H}:C^{2+\epsilon}(M)\to F\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{g}

given by
\mathfrak{H}(f)=\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{f})
. As the composition of two bounded operators, it is also bounded.
Let
K:=\ker\mathfrak{H}\subset C^{2+\epsilon}(M)
. Then
K
is a closed subspace of codimension
g
(the genus of
M
).

Ergodicity.
Suppose that
f\in K
and
\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})}|f(\sigma)|>0
. By Proposition
4.1
,

\varphi\in\operatorname{LG}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
,
\mathcal{L}(\varphi_{f})>0
,

g^{\prime}_{\varphi_{f}}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
and
\mathcal{AS}(\varphi_{f})=0
. As
f\in K
, we additionally have
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{f})=0
. In view of Theorem
8.1
,
this gives the ergodicity of the skew product
T_{\varphi_{f}}
. By Proposition
2.4
, we have the ergodicity of the extended flow
\Phi^{f}_{\mathbb{R}}
.

Reducibility.
Suppose that
f\in K
and
\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})}|f(\sigma)|=0
. By Proposition
4.1
,

\varphi\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
with
\partial_{\pi}(\varphi_{f})=0
and

\varphi_{f}^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
. As
f\in K
, we additionally have
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{f})=0
. In view of Theorem
8.12
,

\varphi_{f}
is a coboundary with a bounded transfer map having at most countably many discontinuities. By Proposition
2.5
, this gives the reducibility of the extended flow
\Phi^{f}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
∎

8.2.4.
Equidistribution of the error in the symmetric case.

We can now conclude also the proof of Theorem
1.3
, by proving that in this case
err_{b}
is uniformely bounded and deducing from ergodicity the equidistribution statement for the singular cocycles as well as the error term.

Proof of the second part of Main Theorem
1.3
.

Suppose that
\psi_{\mathbb{R}}\in\mathcal{U}_{min}
is minimal and satisfies the
. Let
f:M\to{\mathbb{R}}
be any
C^{2+\epsilon}
-observable.

Boundedness of the error.

Let
f_{eb}:M\to{\mathbb{R}}
be the map defined in (
7.30
). By construction (see (
7.31
) and (
7.32
)),
f_{eb}

is a
C^{2+\epsilon}
-map such that
f_{eb}(\sigma)=0
for all
\sigma\in\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})
and
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{f_{eb}})=0
.
By Proposition
4.1
, we know furthermore that
\varphi_{f_{eb}}\in{\operatorname{AC}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
,
\varphi^{\prime}_{f_{eb}}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
and
\partial_{\pi}(\varphi_{f_{eb}})=0
. In view of Proposition
8.12
,

\varphi_{f_{eb}}
is a coboundary with a bounded transfer map having at most countably many discontinuities. By Proposition
2.5
, this gives the reducibility of the extended flow
\Phi^{f_{eb}}_{\mathbb{R}}
, so there exists a continuous map
u:M\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that
\int_{0}^{t}f_{eb}(\psi_{s}x)\,ds=u(x)-u(\psi_{t}x)
. It follows that for every regular
x\in M
and
t>0
we have

|err_{b}(f,t,x)|=\Big|\int_{0}^{t}f_{eb}(\psi_{s}x)\,ds\Big|\leq 2\|u\|_{\sup},

which completes the proof.

Equidistribution of the singular cocycles and the error term.
Assume now in addition that
f\in C^{2+\epsilon}(M)
is not identically zero on
\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})
. We will prove at the same time
err(f,t,x)=\int_{0}^{t}f_{e}(\psi_{\tau}x)d\tau
and
u_{\sigma}(t,x)=\int_{0}^{t}\xi_{\sigma}(\psi_{\tau}x)d\tau
are equidistributed on
{\mathbb{R}}
, in the sense of (
1.9
).

Let
\xi
be respectively
\xi=f_{e}
or
\xi=\xi_{\sigma}
. We want to show that the assumptions of Theorem
8.1
hold for
\varphi_{\xi}
so that we can deduce that the skew product
T_{\varphi_{\xi}}
on
I\times{\mathbb{R}}
is ergodic.
In both cases, by Proposition
4.1
,
\varphi_{\xi},g^{\prime}_{\varphi_{\xi}}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
and (by property
(ii)
, since
\varphi_{\mathbb{R}}\in\mathcal{U}_{min}
)

\mathcal{AS}(\varphi_{\xi})=0
,
\partial_{\pi}(\varphi_{\xi})=0
. We claim furthermore that we also have that
\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})}|\xi(\sigma)|>0
and therefore, also by Proposition
4.1
,
\mathcal{L}(\varphi_{\xi})>0
. To see this for
\xi=f_{e}
, recall that in view of (
7.26
),
\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})}|f_{e}(\sigma)|=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})}|f(\sigma)|>0
.
Furthermore, in view of (
7.27
),
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi_{f_{e}})=0.

For
\xi=\xi_{\sigma}
, on the other hand, recall that, by the definition of
\xi_{\sigma}
and (
7.18
), for every
\sigma\in\mathrm{Fix}(\psi_{\mathbb{R}})
we have
\xi_{\sigma}(\sigma)=1

and
\mathfrak{h}(\xi_{\sigma})=0
.

Thus, since
T
satisfies the
, all assumptions of Theorem
8.1
hold and we conclude that the skew product
T_{\varphi_{\xi}}
on
I\times{\mathbb{R}}
is ergodic. It follows that also the skew product flow
(\Phi_{t}^{\xi})_{t\in{\mathbb{R}}}

on
M\times{\mathbb{R}}
given by

\Phi_{t}^{\xi}(x,r)=\Big(\psi_{t}x,r+\int_{0}^{t}\xi(\psi_{\tau}x)d\tau\Big)

is ergodic.
We now apply the ratio ergodic theorem to the ergodic flow
(\Phi_{t}^{f_{e}})_{t\in{\mathbb{R}}}
and to the characteristic functions of the sets
I\times J_{1}
and
I\times J_{2}
.
Then for a.e.
(x,r)\in I\times{\mathbb{R}}
for any pair of finite intervals
J_{1},J_{2}\subset{\mathbb{R}}
we have

\displaystyle\frac{Leb\{t\in[0,T]:\int_{0}^{t}\xi(\psi_{\tau}x)d\tau\in J_{1}\}}{Leb\{t\in[0,T]:\int_{0}^{t}\xi(\psi_{\tau}x)d\tau\in J_{2}\}}=\frac{\int_{0}^{T}\chi_{I\times(J_{1}+r)}(\Phi_{t}^{\xi}(x,r))\,dt}{\int_{0}^{T}\chi_{I\times(J_{2}+r)}(\Phi_{t}^{\xi}(x,r))\,dt}\to\frac{|J_{1}+r|}{|J_{2}+r|}=\frac{|J_{1}|}{|J_{2}|}.

As
err(f,t,x)=\int_{0}^{t}f_{e}(\psi_{\tau}x)d\tau
and
u_{\sigma}(t,x)=\int_{0}^{t}\xi_{\sigma}(\psi_{\tau}x)d\tau
, this gives the equidistribution
of cocycles
t\mapsto err(f,t,x)
and
t\mapsto u_{\sigma}(t,x)
for a.e.
x\in M
.
∎

Appendix A

In this Appendix we present the proofs of two auxiliary results, the ergodicity criterium (Proposition
8.4
) in §
A.1
and the cohomological reduction to piecewise linear cocycles (Theorem
8.9
) in §
A.2
.

A.1.
Ergodicity criterium

In this Appendix we prove the ergodicity criterium stated as Proposition
8.4
. The proof repeats arguments from the proof of Propositions 5.1, 5.2 in
[
20
]
and is included for convenience.

Proof of Proposition
8.4
.

For simplicity assume that
|I|=1
. First we show that there exists
C>0
such that

(A.1)

|\varphi^{(q_{k})}(x)-\varphi^{(q_{k})}(T^{m}x)|\leq C\text{ for all }0\leq m<p_{k},\ x\in J^{(k)}.

Note that

|\varphi^{(q_{k})}(x)-\varphi^{(q_{k})}(T^{m}x)|=|\varphi^{(m)}(x)-\varphi^{(m)}(T^{q_{k}}x)|\leq\Big|\int_{x}^{T^{q_{k}}x}|(\varphi^{\prime})^{(m)}(y)|\,dy\Big|.

Assume that
g_{\varphi}=0
. In view of (
SUDC2
) in Proposition
5.6
, for every
y\in I^{(k)}
we have

|(\varphi^{\prime})^{(m)}(y)|\leq\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\Big(\frac{|C_{\alpha}^{+}|}{\min_{0\leq i<m}|T^{i}y-l_{\alpha}|}+\frac{|C_{\alpha}^{-}|}{\min_{0\leq i<m}|T^{i}y-r_{\alpha}|}\Big)+M\mathcal{L}(\varphi)\|Q(k)\|.

As
x\in J^{(k)}
, by assumption
(ii)
, there exists
c>0
such that

|T^{i}x-l_{\alpha}|\geq c/q_{k},\ |T^{i}x-r_{\alpha}|\geq c/q_{k},\ |T^{i}(T^{q_{k}}x)-l_{\alpha}|\geq c/q_{k},\ |T^{i}(T^{q_{k}}x)-r_{\alpha}|\geq c/q_{k}

for all
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}
and
0\leq i<p_{k}
. As
x,T^{q_{k}}x\in I^{(k)}
, it follows that

|T^{i}y-l_{\alpha}|\geq c/q_{k},\ |T^{i}y-r_{\alpha}|\geq c/q_{k}\text{ for all }y\in[x,T^{q_{k}}x].

In view of (
3.15
), this gives

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{x}^{T^{q_{k}}x}|(\varphi^{\prime})^{(m)}(y)|\,dy\Big|

\displaystyle\leq|x-T^{q_{k}}x|\mathcal{L}(\varphi)(q_{k}/c+M\|Q(k)\|)

\displaystyle\leq|I^{(k)}|\|Q(k)\|(M+1/c)\mathcal{L}(\varphi)\leq\kappa(M+1/c)\mathcal{L}(\varphi).

Suppose that
g_{\varphi}\neq 0
. As
x,T^{q_{k}}x\in I^{(k)}
, we have that
\{T^{i}[x,T^{q_{k}}x]:0\leq i<m\}
is a tower of intervals.
Hence

|g_{\varphi}^{(m)}(x)-g_{\varphi}^{(m)}(T^{q_{k}}x)|\leq\sum_{0\leq i<m}|g_{\varphi}(T^{i}x)|-g_{\varphi}(T^{i}(T^{q_{k}}x))|\leq\operatorname{Var}g_{\varphi}.

This gives (
A.1
). Therefore, for every
0\leq i<p_{k}
we have

\int_{T^{i}J^{(k)}}|\varphi^{(q_{k})}(x)|\,dx\leq\int_{J^{(k)}}|\varphi^{(q_{k})}(x)|\,dx+|J^{(k)}|C=\int_{J^{(k)}}|S(k)\varphi(x)|\,dx+|J^{(k)}|C.

Hence

\int_{\Xi_{k}}|\varphi^{(q_{k})}(x)|\,dx\leq p_{k}\int_{I^{(k)}}|\varphi^{(q_{k})}(x)|\,dx+p_{k}|J^{(k)}|C=\frac{1}{|I^{(k)}|}\int_{I^{(k)}}|S(k)\varphi(x)|\,dx+C.

In view of assumption
(i)
, this gives the left condition in (
8.1
).

For every
0\leq l<p_{k}
let
[a_{l},b_{l}]=J^{(k)}_{l}
. Repeating some integration by parts arguments from the proof of Proposition 5.2 in
[
20
]
,
we have

\Big|\int_{J^{(k)}_{l}}e^{2\pi s\varphi^{(q_{k})}(x)}\,dx\Big|\leq\frac{1}{|s|}\Big(\frac{2}{\min_{x\in[a_{l},b_{l}]}|(\varphi^{\prime})^{(q_{k})}(x)|}+\operatorname{Var}|_{[a_{l},b_{l}]}\frac{1}{(\varphi^{\prime})^{(q_{k})}}\Big).

In view of
(iii)
, it follows that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{J^{(k)}_{l}}e^{2\pi s\varphi^{(q_{k})}(x)}\,dx\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|s|}\Big(\frac{2}{cq_{k}}+\frac{1}{c^{2}q_{k}^{2}}\operatorname{Var}|_{[a_{l},b_{l}]}(\varphi^{\prime})^{(q_{k})}\Big)\leq\frac{1}{|s|}\Big(\frac{2}{cq_{k}}+\frac{1}{c^{2}q_{k}^{2}}\sum_{0\leq i<q_{k}}\operatorname{Var}|_{T^{i}[a_{l},b_{l}]}\varphi^{\prime}\Big).

By
(ii)
,
\{T^{i}[a_{l},b_{l}]:0\leq i<q_{k}\}
is a tower of intervals and each level interval
T^{i}[a_{l},b_{l}]
is distant from the set
End(T)
by at least

c/q_{k}
. Recall that

\varphi^{\prime}(x)=-\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{C_{\alpha}^{+}}{\{x-l_{\alpha}\}}+\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{C_{\alpha}^{-}}{\{r_{\alpha}-x\}}+g^{\prime}_{\varphi}(x).

Moreover,

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq i<q_{k}}\operatorname{Var}|_{T^{i}[a_{l},b_{l}]}\frac{1}{\{x-l_{\alpha}\}}=\operatorname{Var}_{[c/q_{k},1]}\frac{1}{x}\leq\frac{q_{k}}{c},

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq i<q_{k}}\operatorname{Var}|_{T^{i}[a_{l},b_{l}]}\frac{1}{\{r_{\alpha}-x\}}=\operatorname{Var}_{[0,1-c/q_{k},1]}\frac{1}{1-x}\leq\frac{q_{k}}{c}

and

\sum_{0\leq i<q_{k}}\operatorname{Var}|_{T^{i}[a_{l},b_{l}]}g^{\prime}_{\varphi}\leq\operatorname{Var}g^{\prime}_{\varphi}.

It follows that for every
0\leq l<p_{k}
,

\Big|\int_{J^{(k)}_{l}}e^{2\pi s\varphi^{(q_{k})}(x)}\,dx\Big|\leq\frac{1}{|s|}\Big(\frac{2}{cq_{k}}+\frac{1}{c^{2}q_{k}^{2}}\Big(\mathcal{L}(\varphi)\frac{q_{k}}{c}+\operatorname{Var}(g^{\prime}_{\varphi})\Big)\Big).

As

Leb\Big(\Xi_{k}\setminus\bigcup_{0\leq l<p_{k}}J^{(k)}_{l}\Big)=\sum_{0\leq l<p_{k}}Leb(T^{l}J^{(k)}\setminus J^{(k)}_{l})\leq\frac{2}{3}\sum_{0\leq l<p_{k}}|T^{l}J^{(k)}|=\frac{2}{3}Leb(\Xi_{k}),

this yields

\Big|\int_{\Xi_{k}}e^{2\pi s\varphi^{(q_{k})}(x)}\,dx\Big|\leq\frac{2}{3}Leb(\Xi_{k})+\frac{1}{|s|}\Big(\frac{2}{c}+\frac{1}{c^{2}}\Big(\frac{\mathcal{L}(\varphi)}{c}+\operatorname{Var}(g^{\prime}_{\varphi})\Big)\Big),

which gives the right condition in (
8.1
). By Proposition
8.2
, we have the ergodicity of
T_{\varphi}
.
∎

A.2.
Cohomological reduction

In this Appendix we present the proof of the cohomological reduction stated as Theorem
8.9
. We will assume throughout that
T
satisfies the
. For simplicity will also assume that
|I|=1
. Let us denote by

\operatorname{AC}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha}):=\{\varphi\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha}),\ \text{such\ that}\ \varphi^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})\ \mathrm{and}\ \mathfrak{h}(\varphi^{\prime})=0\}.

Outline of the proof.

We will show first of all that every
\varphi\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
with
\varphi^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
can be modified by a piecewise linear map such that its modification is in
\operatorname{AC}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
, by showing that one can subtract a map whose derivative is
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi^{\prime})
(see Steps 1 and 2 of the proof of Theorem
8.9
below).
The next step of the proof is to apply the correction by a piecewise constant function described in §
6
(see Step 3 of the proof of Theorem
8.9
). We then show that, after this further correction, the resulting map
\widetilde{\varphi}
is a coboundary. We will show more precisely that
\|S(k)\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{\sup}
decays exponentially (see Theorem
A.1
).
Then standard arguments based on decompositions of Birkhoff sums
(see §
7.1.2
) and the Gottschalk-Hedlund theorem yield that
\widetilde{\varphi}
is a coboundary (see Step 4 of the proof of Theorem
8.9
).

The proof of Theorem
A.1
(namely of exponential decay of
\|S(k)\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{\sup}
) is similar to the proof of
Theorem
6.1
in §
6
, or more precisely to the the proof of sub-exponential growth of
\|S(k)\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}/|I^{(k)}|
(see in particular
(
6.4
) in the statement of Theorem
6.1
).
One of the key arguments in this proof was showing that

\mathcal{LV}(S(k)\varphi)
was bounded (or had sub-exponential growth in the non-symmetric case). Here, we will have a stronger input, namely the exponential decay of
\mathcal{LV}(S(k)\varphi)
: indeed,
for every
\varphi\in\operatorname{AC}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
,
since
\varphi
is piecewise absolutely continuous, we have that
\mathcal{LV}(S(k)\varphi)=\operatorname{Var}(S(k)\varphi)
and therefore,
in view of
Theorem
6.1
(applied to
\varphi^{\prime}
) and the control of the
L^{1}
norm via
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{LV}}
given by (
4.3
), for every

k\geq 1
,

(A.2)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{LV}(S(k)\varphi)=\operatorname{Var}&(S(k)\varphi)=\|S(k)({\varphi^{\prime}})\|_{L^{1}(I^{(k)})}\leq C|I^{(k)}|C^{\prime}_{k}(T)\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{LV}}.\end{split}

Since
|I^{(k)}|
decays exponentially, this shows that
\mathcal{LV}(S(k)\varphi)
decays exponentially.
Exploiting this exponential decay, analyzing its effect on all inequalities used in §
6
, we will prove
the exponential decay of
\|S(k)\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{\sup}
. Differently than in §
6
, though, instead of the
L^{1}
-norm, we have now to always use the
\sup
-norm. This requires a detailed and patient analysis of all steps used in §
6
in this new context, which is performed for example in the proofs of Lemmas
A.2
and
A.3
below.

We begin by stating and proving the following exponential decay result.

Theorem A.1
.

Assume that
T
satisfies the
. Suppose that
\varphi\in\operatorname{AC}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
,
\partial_{\pi}(\varphi)=0
and
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi)=0
. Then

\|S(k){\varphi}\|_{\sup}=O(e^{-\lambda k}).

The proof of Theorem
A.1
will follow from combining the following three Lemmas (Lemma
A.2
, Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
A.4
). The first is an improved estimate of the growth of the image
P^{(k)}\varphi
of the correcting operators
P^{(k)}
(introduced in §
6
) when
\varphi\in\operatorname{AC}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(0)}_{\alpha})
and
\partial_{\pi^{(0)}}(\varphi)=0
.

Lemma A.2
.

The correcting operator
P^{(k)}:\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})\to\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}

is such that, for every
\varphi\in\operatorname{AC}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(0)}_{\alpha})
with
\partial_{\pi^{(0)}}(\varphi)=0
,

(A.3)

\|P^{(k)}(S(k)\varphi)\|_{\sup/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}}\leq C\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{LV}}\,W_{k},\quad\text{where}\quad W_{k}:=\sum_{r\geq k}\|Q_{s}(k,r+1)\|\|Z(r+1)\||I^{(r)}|C^{\prime}_{r}(T).

Proof.

Recall that
{P}^{(k)}
is given by
{P}^{(k)}={U}^{(k)}\circ P_{0}^{(k)}-\Delta^{(k)}
.
Let us first give a preliminary estimate for the modifying operator
\Delta^{(k)}:\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})\to H(\pi^{(k)})/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}
starting from the definition of
\Delta^{(k)}
as the series given by (
6.20
).
Let
\varphi\in\operatorname{AC}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(0)}_{\alpha})
with
\partial_{\pi^{(0)}}(\varphi)=0
.

Step 1. Estimates of
\Delta^{(k)}\varphi
.
To estimate the series (
6.20
) (with
S(k)(\varphi)
instead than
\varphi
), for each fixed
r\geq k
we need to estimate

(S_{\flat}(k,r+1))^{-1}\circ U^{(r+1)}\circ\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(r+1)}\circ S(r,r+1)\circ P_{0}^{(r)}\circ S(r)(\varphi).

Let us start from right to left, by estimating first the action of
P_{0}^{(r)}
on
S(r)(\varphi)
, then that one of
\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(r+1)}\circ S(r,r+1)
and finally applying and estimating
(S_{\flat}(k,r+1))^{-1}\circ U^{(r+1)}
.

Step 1 (i). The action of
P_{0}^{(r)}
.
Recall that (by Definition
8
)
P_{0}^{(r)}=I-p_{H(\pi^{(r)})}\circ{\mathcal{M}}^{(r)}
, where
I
is the identity operator and
\mathcal{M}^{(r)}
the preliminary correction given by subtracting the mean in each
I^{(r)}_{\alpha}
.
Since
S(r)(\varphi)\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(r)}_{\alpha})
is piecewise absolutely continuous,

\|S(r)(\varphi)-\mathcal{M}^{(r)}(S(r)(\varphi))\|_{\sup}\leq\operatorname{Var}(S(r)(\varphi)).

Using first this estimate together with the control of the projection by the boundary operator
\|h-p_{H(\pi^{(r)})}h\|\leq C_{\mathcal{G}}\|\partial_{\pi}^{(r)}h\|
given by Lemma
3.4
(see in particular (
3.14
)),
then the comparision between
\partial_{\pi^{(r)}}
and
\partial_{\pi^{(r)}}\circ\mathcal{M}^{(r)}
given by
(
4.23
) and finally the estimate (
A.2
) of the variance together with the invariance of the boundary (
5.7
) and the assumption that
\partial_{\pi^{(r)}}(S(r)\varphi)=\partial_{\pi^{(0)}}(\varphi)=0
,
we get the following chain of inequalities:

(A.4)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|P_{0}^{(r)}\circ S(r)(\varphi)\|_{\sup}&\leq\|S(r)(\varphi)-\mathcal{M}^{(r)}(S(r)(\varphi))\|_{\sup}+\|\mathcal{M}^{(r)}(S(r)(\varphi))-p_{H(\pi^{(r)})}\mathcal{M}^{(r)}(S(r)(\varphi))\|_{\sup}\\
&\leq\operatorname{Var}(S(r)(\varphi))+C_{\mathcal{G}}\|\partial_{\pi^{(r)}}\mathcal{M}^{(r)}(S(r)(\varphi))\|\\
&\leq(1+2dC_{\mathcal{G}})\operatorname{Var}(S(r)(\varphi))+C_{\mathcal{G}}\|\partial_{\pi^{(r)}}(S(r)\varphi)\|\\
&\leq C^{\prime}|I^{(r)}|C^{\prime}_{r}(T)\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{LV}}.\end{split}

Step 1(ii). The action of

\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(r+1)}\circ S(r,r+1)
.

In view of the initial correction estimates of Lemma
6.4
(in particular (
6.8
)), the
L^{1}
-norm of special Birkhoff sums estimate (
5.2
) and the interval lenght control in terms of cocycle matrix norms given by (
3.5
), for every
\phi\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(r)}_{\alpha})
,

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(r+1)}}\circ S(r,r+1)\phi\|\leq\frac{\kappa\sqrt{d}}{|I^{(r+1)}|}\|S(r,r+1)\phi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(r+1)})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa\sqrt{d}}{|I^{(r+1)}|}\|\phi\|_{L^{1}(I^{(r)})}\leq\kappa\sqrt{d}\frac{|I^{(r)}|}{|I^{(r+1)}|}\|\phi\|_{\sup}\leq\kappa\sqrt{d}\|Z(r+1)\|\|\phi\|_{\sup}.

Step 1(iii). The action of
(S_{\flat}(k,r+1))^{-1}\circ U^{(r+1)}
.
Since
\|{U}^{(r+1)}\|=1
, by (
A.4
), this gives (applied to
\phi=P_{0}^{(r)}\circ S(r)(\varphi)
)

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle(S_{\flat}(k,r+1))^{-1}\circ{U}^{(r+1)}\circ{\mathcal{M}_{H}^{(r+1)}}\circ S(r,r+1)\circ P_{0}^{(r)}\circ S(k,r)(S(k)\varphi)\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\sqrt{d}C^{\prime}\|Q_{s}(k,r+1)\|\|Z(r+1)\||I^{(r)}|C^{\prime}_{r}(T)\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{LV}}.

As
\Delta^{(k)}(S(k)\varphi)
is the sum of the series (
6.20
), it follows that

(A.5)

\|\Delta^{(k)}(S(k)\varphi)\|_{\sup/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}}\leq\kappa\sqrt{d}C^{\prime}W_{k}\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{LV}}.

Step 2. Estimates of
{P}^{(k)}\varphi
.
We can now estimate
{P}^{(k)}:\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})\to\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}
recalling that
{P}^{(k)}={U}^{(k)}\circ P_{0}^{(k)}-\Delta^{(k)}
.
As
\|U^{(k)}\|=1
, in view of (
A.4
), if
\varphi\in\operatorname{AC}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
and
\partial_{\pi}(\varphi)=0
then

\displaystyle\|U^{(k)}\circ P_{0}^{(k)}(S(k)\varphi)\|_{\sup/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}}

\displaystyle\leq\|P_{0}^{(k)}\circ S(k)(\varphi)\|_{\sup}\leq C^{\prime}|I^{(k)}|C^{\prime}_{k}(T)\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{LV}}\leq C^{\prime}W_{k}\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{LV}}.

Together with (
A.5
), this gives the desired estimate and proves the Lemma. ∎

Lemma A.3
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
A.1
, for any
k\geq 0
,

\|S(k){\varphi}\|_{\sup}\leq C\big(\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{LV}}\,V_{k}+\|Q_{s}(k)\|\|\varphi\|_{\sup}\big),

where
V_{k}
is given by the following series

\displaystyle V_{k}=\sum_{0\leq l\leq k}\|Q_{s}(l,k)\|\big(W_{l}+\|Z(l)\|W_{l-1}\big),

in which
W_{-1}:=0
by convention and the series
W_{l}
for
l\geq 0
is defined in (
A.3
) of Lemma
A.2
.

Proof.

By the definition of the operator
\mathfrak{h}
(see (
6.29
)), since
\mathfrak{h}(\varphi)=0
, we have that
U^{(0)}(\varphi)=P^{(0)}(\varphi)
.
In view of the equivariance described by Lemma
6.7
, it follows that

{U}^{(k)}\circ S(k){\varphi}=S_{\flat}(k)\circ{U}^{(0)}{\varphi}=S_{\flat}(k)\circ{P}^{(0)}\varphi={P}^{(k)}\circ S(k)\varphi.

Therefore, by Lemma
A.2
, we have

\|{U}^{(k)}\circ S(k){\varphi}\|_{\sup/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}}=\|P^{(k)}(S(k)\varphi)\|_{\sup/\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}}\leq CW_{k}\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{LV}}.

It follows that for every
k\geq 0
there exists
\varphi_{k}\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I^{(k)}_{\alpha})
and

s_{k}\in\Gamma^{(k)}_{s}
such that

(A.6)

\displaystyle S(k){\varphi}=\varphi_{k}+s_{k}\text{ and }\|\varphi_{k}\|_{\sup}\leq CW_{k}\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{LV}}.

Setting
s_{0}:=\Delta s_{0}
and
\Delta s_{k+1}=s_{k+1}-Z(k+1)s_{k}
for any
k\geq 1
, since for
s_{k}\in\Gamma^{(k)}
we have that
S(k,k+1)s_{k}=Z(k+1)s_{k}
, we get

\Delta s_{k+1}=s_{k+1}-S(k,k+1)s_{k}=(S(k+1)\varphi-\varphi_{k+1})-S(k,k+1)(S(k)\varphi-\varphi_{k})=-\varphi_{k+1}+S(k,k+1)\varphi_{k}.

Therefore, by
(
A.6
),

\displaystyle\|\Delta s_{k+1}\|_{\sup}

\displaystyle=\|\varphi_{k+1}-S(k,k+1)\varphi_{k}\|_{\sup}\leq\|\varphi_{k+1}\|_{\sup}+\|S(k,k+1)\varphi_{k}\|_{\sup}

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi_{k+1}\|_{\sup}+\|Z(k+1)\|\|\varphi_{k}\|_{\sup}\leq C(W_{k+1}+\|Z(k+1)\|W_{k})\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{LV}}

and, since by definition
\Delta s_{0}=s_{0}=\varphi-\varphi_{0}
,

\|\Delta s_{0}\|_{\sup}=\|\varphi-\varphi_{0}\|_{\sup}\leq\|\varphi\|_{\sup}+CW_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{LV}}.

Since
s_{k}=\sum_{0\leq l\leq k}Q(l,k)\Delta s_{l}
and
\Delta s_{l}\in\Gamma^{(l)}_{s}
, setting
W_{-1}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\|s_{k}\|_{\sup}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{0\leq l\leq k}\|Q(l,k)\Delta s_{l}\|_{\sup}\leq\sum_{0\leq l\leq k}\|Q_{s}(l,k)\|\|\Delta s_{l}\|_{\sup}

\displaystyle\leq\|Q_{s}(k)\|\|{\varphi}\|_{\sup}+C\sum_{0\leq l\leq k}\|Q_{s}(l,k)\|(W_{l}+\|Z(l)\|W_{l-1})\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{LV}}.

In view of (
A.6
), it follows that

\displaystyle\|S(k){\varphi}\|_{\sup}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\varphi}_{k}\|_{\sup}+\|s_{k}\|\leq\|Q_{s}(k)\|\|{\varphi}\|_{\sup}+2C\sum_{0\leq l\leq k}\|Q_{s}(l,k)\|(W_{l}+\|Z(l)\|W_{l-1})\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{LV}},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma A.4
.

Suppose that
T
satisfies the
. Then for every
0<\tau<(\lambda_{1}-\lambda)/5
we have

V_{k}=O(e^{-\lambda k})
.

Proof.

In view of (
3.28
) in Proposition
3.9
and
|I^{(r)}|\leq\kappa\|Q(r)\|^{-1}=O(e^{-\lambda_{1}r})
(see (
3.15
) and (
UDC3
)), we have

\displaystyle W_{k}

\displaystyle=O\Big(\sum_{r\geq k}\|Q_{s}(k,r+1)\|\|Z(r+1)\||I^{(r)}|C^{\prime}_{r}(T)\Big)

\displaystyle=O\Big(\sum_{r\geq k}e^{-\lambda(r+1-k)}e^{4\tau r}e^{-\lambda_{1}r}\Big)=O\Big(e^{-(\lambda_{1}-4\tau)k}\sum_{r\geq k}e^{-(\lambda+\lambda_{1}-4\tau)(r-k)}\Big)=O\big(e^{-(\lambda_{1}-4\tau)k}).

By the definition of
V_{k}
, it follows that,

\displaystyle V_{k}

\displaystyle=O\Big(\sum_{0\leq l\leq k}\|Q_{s}(l,k)\|\big(e^{-(\lambda_{1}-4\tau)l}+\|Z(l)\|e^{-(\lambda_{1}-4\tau)(l-1)}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=O\Big(\sum_{0\leq l\leq k}e^{-\lambda(k-l)}e^{\tau l}e^{-(\lambda_{1}-4\tau)l}\Big)=O\Big(e^{-\lambda k}\sum_{0\leq l\leq k}e^{-(\lambda_{1}-\lambda-5\tau)l}\Big)=O\big(e^{-\lambda k}\big).

∎

Proof of Theorem
A.1
.

The proof follows immediately by combining Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
A.4
, which show that

\|S(k){\varphi}\|_{\sup}\leq C^{\prime}\big(\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{LV}}\,e^{-\lambda k}+\|Q_{s}(k)\|\|\varphi\|_{\sup}\big),

Since also
\|Q_{s}(k)\|=O(e^{-\lambda k})
by the
(see (
UDC1
) of Definition
3
), we get that
\|S(k){\varphi}\|_{\sup}=O(e^{-\lambda k})
.
∎

We can now also prove the cohomological reduction.

Proof of Theorem
8.9
.

Let us assume that
T
satisfies the
and that

\varphi\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
and

\varphi^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{LG}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
.
Fix any
0<\tau<\min\{(\lambda_{1}-\lambda)/5,\lambda\}
.

Step 1. First correction for the derivative to be in the kernel of
\mathfrak{h}
.

Let
h_{1}=\mathfrak{h}(\varphi^{\prime})\in H(\pi)
and take any piecewise linear
\underline{\varphi}\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})

such that
\underline{\varphi}^{\prime}=h_{1}
. Then
\mathfrak{h}((\varphi-\underline{\varphi})^{\prime})=0
.

Step 2. Correction to be in
\operatorname{AC}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
.
Since
\mathfrak{h}((\varphi-\underline{\varphi})^{\prime})=0
by Step 1, Corollary
7.10
shows that the sum of jumps
s(\varphi-\underline{\varphi})=\int_{I}(\varphi-\underline{\varphi})^{\prime}(x)\,dx=0
. By (
4.2
),
it follows that

\sum_{\mathcal{O}\in\Sigma(\pi)}(\partial_{\pi}(\varphi-\underline{\varphi}))_{\mathcal{O}}=0.

Since the image of
\partial_{\pi}
consists of all vectors
(x_{\mathcal{O}})_{\mathcal{O}}
such that
\sum_{\mathcal{O}\in\Sigma(\pi)}x_{\mathcal{O}}=0
(see (
3.12
)), there exists
h_{2}\in\Gamma
such that
\partial_{\pi}(h_{2})=\partial_{\pi}(\varphi-\underline{\varphi})
. We claim that
\varphi-\underline{\varphi}-h_{2}
belongs to
\operatorname{AC}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
. To see this, notice first that
\varphi-\underline{\varphi}-h_{2}\in\operatorname{AC}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
and that
(\varphi-\underline{\varphi}-h_{2})^{\prime}=\varphi^{\prime}-h_{1}\in\operatorname{LG}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
. Furthermore

\displaystyle\partial_{\pi}(\varphi-\underline{\varphi}-h_{2})=0,\quad\mathfrak{h}((\varphi-\underline{\varphi}-h_{2})^{\prime})=\mathfrak{h}((\varphi-\underline{\varphi})^{\prime})=0,

so
\varphi-\underline{\varphi}-h_{2}\in\operatorname{AC}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
.

Step 3. Last correction to be in the kernel of
\mathfrak{h}
.

Let
h_{3}=\mathfrak{h}(\varphi-\underline{\varphi}-h_{2})\in H(\pi)
and set

\widetilde{\varphi}:=\varphi-\underline{\varphi}-h_{2}-h_{3}.

Then
\widetilde{\varphi}\in\operatorname{AC}^{\mathfrak{h}}(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}I_{\alpha})
with

\mathfrak{h}(\widetilde{\varphi})=0
and
\partial_{\pi}(\widetilde{\varphi})=\partial_{\pi}(\varphi-\underline{\varphi}-h_{2})-\partial_{\pi}(h_{3})=0
.

Step 4. Proof that
\widetilde{\varphi}
is a coboundary.

Given any every bounded function
\varphi:I\to{\mathbb{R}}
and
n>0
, by decomposing the Birkhoff sums
\varphi^{(n)}
into special Birkhoff sums (see for example
[
44
, § 2.2.3]
), we can get the estimate

(A.7)

\|\varphi^{(n)}\|_{\sup}\leq 2\sum_{l\in{\mathbb{N}}}\|Z(l+1)\|\|S(l)\varphi\|_{\sup},

As
0<\tau<\lambda
,
in view of the
(in particular the estimate of
\|Z(l)\|
) and Theorem
A.1
, which gives that
\|S(l)\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{\sup}=O(e^{-\lambda l})
, it follows that

\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}\|_{\sup}=O\big(\sum_{l\in{\mathbb{N}}}\|Z(l+1)\|e^{-\lambda l}\big)=O\big(\sum_{l\in{\mathbb{N}}}e^{-(\lambda-\tau)l}\big)=O(1).

Applying Gottschalk-Hedlund type arguments (see
[
44
, §3.4]
), we obtain that

\widetilde{\varphi}
is a coboundary with a bounded transfer map.

Step 5. Conclusive arguments.

Let us now define
\psi:={\varphi}-\widetilde{\varphi}
. By Step 4,
\psi
and
{\varphi}
differ by a coboundary, so they are cohomologous. Furthermore, since by definition of
\psi
and of
\widetilde{\varphi}
(see Step 3)

\psi={\varphi}-\widetilde{\varphi}=\underline{\varphi}+h_{2}+h_{3},

and
\underline{\varphi}
,
h_{2}
and
h_{3}
are all piecewise linear (actually piecewise constant in the case of
h_{2}
and
h_{3}
) functions (by construction, see Step 1 and Step 2),
we see that
\psi
is piecewise linear. Furthermore, since by construction
\mathfrak{h}(\widetilde{\varphi})=0
and
\partial_{\pi}(\widetilde{\varphi})
(in view of Step 3), we have that

\mathfrak{h}(\psi)=\mathfrak{h}({\varphi})-\mathfrak{h}(\widetilde{\varphi})=\mathfrak{h}({\varphi})\quad\text{and}\quad\partial_{\pi}(\psi)=\partial_{\pi}(\varphi)-\partial_{\pi}(\widetilde{\varphi})=\partial_{\pi}(\varphi).

Finally, Theorem
A.1
shows that
\|S(k)({\varphi}-\psi)\|_{\sup}=\|S(k)\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{\sup}
decay exponentially.
This completes the proof.
∎
